React's controlled-input `_valueTracker` swallows a direct `.value` assignment, so onChange never fires and those three verifiers fail even with byte-identical DOM. Either (i) render inspector inputs uncontrolled with a ref + native listener, or (ii) the inspector commit also rewrites those three drivers to use the native value setter. _verify_digest.js is unaffected — it never touches the inspector, so 60/60 can hold across that commit.",
    "designDigest": "### DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited\nCONTRACT:\n// ============================================================================\n// src/core/plugin.ts — the Phase A plugin contract.\n//\n// THE ONE INVARIANT THAT DECIDES EVERY OTHER DECISION:\n//   definePlugin() RETURNS A LEGACY `TYPES` ENTRY.\n//   tests/legacy/_verify_digest.js:181-190 does, verbatim:\n//       const def = TYPES[nd.type];\n//       const refs = def.referenceOnly || [];\n//       const ins  = nodeInputs(nd).map(...)          // -> TYPES[nd.type].ins  (index.html:4050)\n//       let f = def.eval(nd.params, ins, nd);          // MUST return Float32Array\n//       f = propagateFieldMetadata(f, ins, def);       // reads def.fieldSemantics (index.html:3229)\n//   and :154 reads def.params[].key / .def. Every one of those survives byte-for-byte.\n//   THE ORACLE FILE IS NOT EDITED IN ANY PHASE A COMMIT. That is the design goal, not a nicety:\n//   the moment the oracle and the app move in the same commit, \"60/60 green\" stops being evidence.\n//\n// THE MEASUREMENT THAT MAKES ADDITIVE FIELDS FREE (I ran this, it is not an assumption):\n//   Across all 8,112 lines of apps/terrain-studio/index.html there are ZERO occurrences of\n//   Object.keys(def), Object.entries(def), `for (... in def)`, or JSON.stringify(def) over a TYPES\n//   entry. The COMPLETE set of properties ever read off a TYPES entry is 11:\n//       cat(9) name(9+7) ins(4) desc(4) params(3) eval(3) info(2) fieldSemantics(2)\n//       referenceOnly(1) note(1) passthrough(1)\n//   plus `effect`, declared on 7 nodes and read 0 times (measured: /\\.effect\\b/ -> 0 hits).\n//   => An unknown key on a TYPES entry is UNOBSERVABLE by construction. Manifest growth is free.\n// ============================================================================\n\ntype NodeTypeId = string;             // the TYPES key: \"perlin\" | \"hydraulic\" | ... (60 of them)\ntype Field      = Float32Array;       // length is ALWAYS ctx.len === fieldW()*fieldH()\ntype Affine     = [number,number,number,number,number,number] | null;  // XF, index.html:1109\n\n/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   1. THE LEGACY HALF — this is exactly today's entry, no field renamed.\n      Justification per key = \"the oracle or the app reads it, here\".\n   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\ninterface LegacyTypesEntry {\n  cat: 'gen'|'comb'|'filt'|'ero'|'mask'|'data'|'effect'|'out';\n      // 8 values, keyed into CAT at index.html:4036-4038. Read by drawNode/menus/exportish:6793.\n  name: string;\n      // 9 read sites (drawNode:5045, evalGraphProgressive:4872, statOut:4831, ...).\n  ins: readonly string[];\n      // SLOT LABELS. ORDER IS DIGESTED: the oracle wires a DISTINCT perlin seed per slot\n      // (_verify_digest.js:198 SEEDS, :323) precisely so a slot swap moves the digest.\n      // Reordering `ins` is a behaviour change. Reordering `params` is NOT (defaults keyed by .key).\n  desc: string;                          // plain text, 6,732 chars total, zero markup. Panel only.\n  params: readonly ParamDesc[];\n      // pr.key and pr.def ARE INSIDE THE HASH: _verify_digest.js:154 does\n      // `def.params.forEach(pr => { p[pr.key] = cloneParams(pr.def) })`. Changing any `def` value,\n      // any `key`, moves that node's digest. Changing `label`/`fmt`/order does not.\n  eval: (params: Record<string,any>, ins: readonly (Field|null)[], node: GraphNode) => Field;\n      // ***GENERATED BY definePlugin — see §4.*** Signature and return type frozen at today's.\n      // Measured signature census over TYPES (index.html:4068-4651):\n      //   (p,ins) x36   (p,ins,nd) x11   (p) x10   (p,ins,node) x3   = 60.\n  note?: string;                         // 22 nodes. HTML string, panel only.\n  passthrough?: true;                    // 7 nodes: water snow satmap colorerosion weathering\n                                         // satmapblend colormixer. ONE read site: markParamDirty:4781.\n  effect?: string;                       // SAME 7 nodes. Read 0 times today. Promoted in step 8.\n  fieldSemantics?: 'preserve-primary'|'temperature-pair';\n      // 5 nodes (warp:4205 blur:4231 transform:4245 | blend:4173 maxmin:4179).\n      // Read by propagateFieldMetadata:3229-3231, which the ORACLE calls at :190. Load-bearing.\n  referenceOnly?: readonly number[];     // 1 node (drawmask:4096). Oracle reads it at :182.\n  info?: (node: GraphNode) => string;    // 1 node (transform:4270). Reads node._xfMode. Panel only.\n}\n\n/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   2. THE MANIFEST HALF — additive only. Nothing here is read by the oracle,\n      by any evaluator, or by any renderer in Phase A. It is declaration that\n      GATES consume. Provably inert (see the measurement in the header).\n   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n\n// ch27's Standard Map Registry, verbatim wire names\n// (origin/main:terrain-architect/references/27-engine-data-handoff.md, §The Standard Map Registry).\n// ch27 §Naming: \"the registry names above are the wire names; the tool never re-names per engine.\"\ntype MapName =\n  | 'height'\n  | 'moisture' | 'insolation' | 'temperature' | 'windVector'      // climate  (ch27 §Climate layer)\n  | 'soilDepth' | 'strataHardness'                                // geology  (ch27 §Geology layer)\n  | 'wetness' | 'flowVelocity' | 'waterDepth' | 'waterSurface'    // hydrology(ch27 §Hydrology layer)\n  | 'curvature' | 'ao'                                            // geometry (ch27 §Geometry layer)\n  | 'sedimentDepth' | 'sandDepth' | 'snowDepth'                   // layer stack (08:66-77, 18:90)\n  | 'albedo'                                                      // PREVIEW ONLY — ch27 §Masking\n                                                                  // Doctrine forbids baked colour in\n                                                                  // the runtime handoff; it survives\n                                                                  // as a review product only.\n  | 'diagnostics';                                                // non-field scalars (see §3)\n\ninterface Port {\n  map: MapName;\n  kind: 'state' | 'derived' | 'preview';\n      // ch27 §State maps vs derived maps — OPPOSITE lifecycles, and conflating them is the defect\n      // that chapter exists to kill. state = path-dependent, may never be recomputed from final\n      // geometry. derived = pure function of final fields, recomputed never patched.\n      // TODAY, measured: d_slope d_height d_curvature d_occlusion d_flow d_deposits d_wear d_texture\n      // are all `derived` and ch27-COMPLIANT. temperature/windVector/snowDepth/insolation are\n      // `state` and already exist as ports. Nothing else does.\n  unit: 'm'|'°C'|'m/s'|'mm/yr'|'1/m'|'fraction'|'none';\n      // ch14:83-87 requires the unit to live in the descriptor. Today it lives in a `fmt` display\n      // closure (63 of them, 13 closing over `terrainDef`) and is structurally absent from 183 of\n      // 235 param declarations. Declaring it is free; CONSUMING it in an eval is not.\n  vector?: true;                       // RG32F pair (ch27: windVector, flowVelocity). Never\n                                       // normalised, never hue-encoded, never lattice-snapped.\n  legacySlot?: `_${string}`;\n      // ***THE DIGEST BRIDGE.*** Where the generated shim scatters this port so the oracle still\n      // finds it. _verify_digest.js:259-275 reaches aux by DOTTED PATH off the node object:\n      //   d_sunshadow  _solarExposure\n      //   d_temperature _temperatureC _solarShadow _solarExposure\n      //   d_heat        _temperatureC\n      //   d_wind        _wind.u _wind.v _wind.speed\n      //   d_windmodify  _wind.u _wind.v _wind.speed\n      //   snow          _snowLayer.depthM  (+7 float64 scalars, :271-275)\n      //   satmap        _driver\n      // Keep the name and the ARRAY IDENTITY and the digest cannot move.\n  legacyOpaque?: true;\n      // Phase A escape hatch for the two ports whose legacy slot is an OBJECT, not a field:\n      // `_wind` {u,v,speed,encoded,...} and `_snowLayer` {depthM,volumeM3,...}. Decomposing them\n      // into ch27 ports requires editing the oracle's dotted paths — that is Phase B, not Phase A.\n}\n\n// ch27 §The co-evolution rule, transcribed from its node table. Machine-readable on purpose.\ntype Ch27Process = 'hydraulic'|'thermal'|'aeolian'|'glacial'|'soil-production'|'orographic'|'snow';\n\ninterface PluginSpec extends Omit<LegacyTypesEntry,'eval'> {\n  id: NodeTypeId;                      // the TYPES key, now carried BY the entry, not only above it.\n\n  /* --- what this node consumes, typed (ch27 §First-Class Citizen Rule: typed ports) --- */\n  inPorts: readonly {\n    label: string;                     // MUST equal ins[i] — asserted; `ins` stays the source of truth\n    accepts: MapName | 'any';          // d_heat slot0 accepts 'temperature'; snow slot1/2 likewise.\n                                       // Today this is enforced at RUNTIME by temperatureCFromField()\n                                       // returning null and the node writing nd._inputError\n                                       // (index.html:4461, 4505, 4592-4594). Declaring it lets the\n                                       // EDITOR refuse the edge instead of the node failing quietly.\n    referenceOnly?: true;              // mirrors LegacyTypesEntry.referenceOnly; drawmask slot 0\n  }[];\n\n  /* --- what this node PRODUCES. This is the co-evolution contract. --- */\n  writes: readonly Port[];\n      // MUST be complete and MUST be exact: the shim throws if the returned object's key set is not\n      // identical to writes.map(p => p.map). Missing => throw. Extra => throw.\n      // This is what kills `hydroMassDiag` (index.html:1443, a module-level last-writer-wins global\n      // written at :1495 AND :3906 — two erosion nodes in one graph and the first budget is gone).\n      // The mass budget now has a declared home or the node cannot ship.\n  process?: Ch27Process;\n      // Declares WHICH physical process this node models, so the static gate can look up ch27's\n      // required co-updates. `undefined` = not a physical process = exempt.\n\n  /* --- locality & resolution policy (ch14:38-44, ch14:85-86, ch14:108-111) --- */\n  locality: 'local'|'neighbourhood'|'global';\n      // local = per-pixel (the 19 EXACT_TYPES value ops); neighbourhood = reads a bounded ring\n      // (blur, warp, thermal); global = whole-field reduction or routing (histeq, priorityFloodFill,\n      // d8Accumulation, ridged/worley/gradient self-normalise). ch27 §Tiling: GLOBAL fields must be\n      // sliced from a global solve, never computed per tile. Declaring it is what makes that\n      // checkable later.\n  resolutionPolicy: 'invariant' | { bound: 'cells'; scaleBy: 'resScale' } | { bound: 'iterations' };\n      // ch14:85-86's `cells⚠` class, which EXISTS but is undeclared today. Measured: 4 params are\n      // raw cell counts multiplied by resScale() at dispatch (blur.radius:4233, d_deposits.radius:4554,\n      // d_peaks.radius:4561, sculpt.radius via sculptField:1581); hydraulic.radius:4359 is literally\n      // formatted \" px\"; 10 more are iteration counts (ch14:108-111 calls that a smell).\n      // Counter-example done right: d_occlusion.radius:4547 is a FRACTION of n and is invariant.\n      // NOTE: moving the resScale() call out of the eval into dispatch is NOT free — the rounding\n      // `Math.max(1,Math.round(p.radius*resScale()))` is inside the digest. Declare now, move later.\n\n  /* --- capabilities the runtime must grant, replacing hardcoded lists --- */\n  exactUnderTransform?: true;\n      // REPLACES the hand-maintained `EXACT_TYPES` Set at index.html:4048-4049 (19 type-id strings\n      // living OUTSIDE the registry). The runtime derives\n      //   EXACT_TYPES = new Set(PLUGINS.filter(p => p.exactUnderTransform).map(p => p.id))\n      // and a gate asserts the derived Set is === the literal one. Without this, Phase B's\n      // \"plugin per node\" leaves a hardcoded 19-name type list behind in the core, which defeats it.\n  reentrant?: true;\n      // EXACTLY ONE node: transform (index.html:4258-4269). It calls inputEdge(), exactChain(),\n      // and evalExact() from inside its own eval, under a mutated global XF. This is the only node\n      // that cannot be a pure (params,inputs)->outputs function. Declaring it means the runtime\n      // grants ctx.evaluateUpstream(); everything else gets a ctx WITHOUT it.\n  gpuVariant?: 'square-only';\n      // 6 nodes branch on gpuReady() inside eval (perlin:4072 simplex:4076 ridged:4080 warp:4208\n      // thermal:4298 hydraulic:4363), + erosion2 via erosion2Field:3938. gpuReady() is\n      // `USE_GPU && terrainDef.lattice!==\"hex\" && GPU.init()` (index.html:1502) — the lattice\n      // predicate IS the gate, because every GPU kernel taps ivec2 ±(1,0) and samples x/uN, which on\n      // hex is the half-cell zig-zag. NOTE: _verify_digest.js:110 pins USE_GPU=false, so the 60/60\n      // gate covers ONLY the CPU branch. _verify_gpu.js is the gate for this flag.\n\n  /* --- invalidation, split into the two facets the code already disagrees about --- */\n  paramsAffectHeight: boolean;         // false for the 7 `passthrough:true` nodes (markParamDirty:4781)\n  paramsAffectColour: boolean;         // false ONLY for water and snow — the evaluators' second,\n      // INCONSISTENT rule: `if(nd.type!==\"water\"&&nd.type!==\"snow\")colorDirty=true`\n      // at index.html:4848 AND :4883. Two classifications (7 types vs 2) of one concept, both\n      // load-bearing. PHASE A REPRODUCES BOTH EXACTLY AND UNIFIES NEITHER — the digest always\n      // evaluates cold (_verify_digest.js:156 sets _dirty:true on every node) and therefore CANNOT\n      // see a wrong unification. It would ship silently.\n\n  /* --- the modern eval. definePlugin wraps this; nobody calls it directly. --- */\n  compute: (params: Record<string,any>,\n            ports:  Readonly<Record<string, Field|null>>,   // keyed by inPorts[].label\n            ctx:    EvalContext,\n            node:   GraphNode) => Outputs;                  // see §3\n}\n\n/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   3. OUTPUTS — ch27's multi-map return, plus scalars.\n   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\ntype Outputs = {\n  height?: Field;                      // the primary. Optional so a pure data-map node need not lie.\n} & Partial<Record<Exclude<MapName,'height'|'diagnostics'>, Field | unknown>>\n  & { diagnostics?: Record<string, number> };\n      // `diagnostics` admits SCALARS, not fields, and it is not optional decoration: the oracle\n      // digests 7 float64 scalars off snow (_verify_digest.js:271-275 — volumeM3 depositedM3\n      // meltedM3 movedM3 maxDepthM coverage iterationsRun). ch27 §Verification demands a closed\n      // budget for every conserved quantity, so the contract must admit scalars or the budget goes\n      // back to a module global. It already did once: hydroMassDiag.\n\n/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   4. definePlugin — the whole trick is 12 lines.\n   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\nexport function definePlugin(spec: PluginSpec): LegacyTypesEntry & { plugin: PluginSpec } {\n  assertPortsMatchIns(spec);           // inPorts[i].label === ins[i], same length, same order\n  const slots = spec.writes.filter(p => p.legacySlot);\n  return {\n    ...spec,                           // cat/name/ins/desc/params/note/passthrough/effect/\n                                       // fieldSemantics/referenceOnly/info pass through UNTOUCHED\n    plugin: spec,\n    // ── THE SHIM. Legacy signature in, legacy Float32Array out. ──────────────\n    eval(params, ins, node) {\n      const ports = bindPorts(spec, ins);        // positional -> named. PASSES ins[i] BY REFERENCE.\n      const out   = spec.compute(params, ports, CTX, node);\n      assertWritesExact(spec, out);              // key set === writes. Missing OR extra => throw.\n      for (const p of slots) node[p.legacySlot!] = (out as any)[p.map] ?? null;\n      return (out.height ?? ports[spec.ins[0]] ?? CTX.newField()) as Field;\n    },\n  };\n}\nexport const TYPES = Object.fromEntries(PLUGINS.map(p => [p.plugin.id, p]));\n\n// WHY A SHIM AND NOT A NEW EVAL SIGNATURE — this is the single most important choice here.\n// Changing eval's RETURN TYPE to {height, aux} would require editing four call sites, and one of\n// them is _verify_digest.js:189. That makes the oracle and the app move in the same commit, which\n// is the one thing a byte-identity gate must never do. The shim buys the entire modern contract —\n// named ports, declared multi-map outputs, a context object, runtime completeness checking — while\n// `def.eval(nd.params, ins, nd) -> Float32Array` stays literally true. Cost: one closure call per\n// node evaluation. The shim can be deleted in Phase B, or never; it is not in a hot loop.\n//\n// FOUR IDENTITY RULES THE SHIM MUST OBEY (each protects a measured, real invariant):\n//   (a) bindPorts passes ins[i] BY REFERENCE. No copy, no wrap, no subarray. FIELD_META\n//       (index.html:3202) is a WeakMap keyed on ARRAY IDENTITY; a copy silently drops every °C tag\n//       and tempmask/d_heat/snow/water fall into their \"Connect a Temperature field\" branches\n//       (:4461,:4505,:4592-4594) — 6+ digests move from what looks like plumbing.\n//   (b) The returned height is out.height ITSELF, not a copy. Four caches are keyed by array\n//       reference identity (_colorErosionRoute:5978, _weatherSignal:6031, _biomeMaskCache:6094,\n//       curField/curSnowLayerRef:6819-6822). A fresh array per call is a silent 5-50x regression\n//       the digest passes cleanly.\n//   (c) The aux scatter writes the SAME object the compute produced, at the SAME `_name`.\n//   (d) The fallback chain `out.height ?? ins[0] ?? newField()` reproduces the exact\n//       `ins[0]||newField()` idiom the 7 passthrough evals use today (e.g. satmapblend:4642).\nEVAL CONTEXT:\n// ============================================================================\n// src/core/context.ts — EvalContext.\n//\n// THE DECISION THAT MAKES THIS DIGEST-SAFE: in Phase A the context is a LIVE VIEW\n// over the existing module bindings — every property is a getter — NOT a snapshot.\n// ============================================================================\n\nexport const CTX: EvalContext = {\n  get res()       { return RES },            // index.html:972   `let RES=512`\n  get rows()      { return latticeRows(RES) },//            :1066 fieldH()\n  get len()       { return fieldLen() },     //              :1067\n  get lattice()   { return terrainDef.lattice },//           :6133\n  get isHex()     { return isHex() },        //              :6142\n  get rowPitch()  { return isHex() ? HEX_ROW : 1 },// :6143 — the `isHex()?HEX_ROW:1` idiom, 10 sites\n  get cellSizeM() { return cellSizeM() },    //              :6135  terrainDef.scale/RES\n  get resScale()  { return resScale() },     //              :1221  SCALE_RES ? RES/REF_RES : 1\n  get quality()   { return BUILD_QUALITY },  //              :1222\n  get world()     { return terrainDef },     //              :6133  LIVE object, never a spread\n  get xf()        { return XF },             //              :1109\n  get gpu()       { return gpuReady() ? GPU : null },//      :1502\n  parityShift(y)  { return terrainDef.lattice===\"hex\" ? 0.5*(y&1) : 0 },  // the `hxs` idiom, 8 sites\n  neighbours(y)   { return hexNb(y) },       //              :6174  D6 odd-r one-ring\n  newField(v)     { return newField(v) },    //              :1068\n  sample(f,x,y)   { return sampleBilinear(f,x,y) },//        :1069\n  meta(f)         { return fieldMetadata(f) },//             :3216  FIELD_META WeakMap, :3202\n  tag(f,m)        { return tagTemperatureField(f, m.temperatureC, m) },// :3209 returns the SAME array\n  hash(x,y,s)     { return hash2(x,y,s) },   //              :1128\n  withResolution, withTransform, evaluateUpstream,           // see below\n}\n\n// ── WHY GETTERS AND NOT A SNAPSHOT ──────────────────────────────────────────\n// TWO of the four mutable hidden inputs are WRITTEN DURING EVALUATION:\n//   RES, by atFeatureScale (index.html:2606-2616). Line 2611 is literally\n//       `const prev=RES; let out; RES=w; try{out=fn(small);} finally{RES=prev;}`\n//   with the comment `// the kernels read the global RES`. It is dynamic scoping and it is\n//   load-bearing for erosion2 (:3938), thermal (:4298) and hydraulic (:4373).\n//   XF, by transform's own eval (index.html:4263-4264), around a recursive evalExact() of its own\n//   upstream cone.\n// A frozen `ctx.res` captured at dispatch would make every node reached through atFeatureScale run\n// at full RES instead of RES/k — a digest drift on erosion2 in the same commit (and erosion2\n// GENUINELY fires at the digest's 256²: erosionScale ships 0.38, featureScale = lerp(1,7,0.38) =\n// 3.28 at index.html:3924, and 3.28 > 1.02, so the RES-mutation branch executes under the gate).\n// Because CTX.res is a getter over the same binding, the rebinding still propagates. Behaviour is\n// bit-identical because the storage is literally the same storage.\n//\n// This also DISSOLVES the leak the survey found rather than reproducing it. simulateTerrainWind\n// (index.html:3444-3445) already accepts a context — `const n=context.res||RES, def=context.terrainDef||terrainDef`\n// — and then at :3465 reads the GLOBAL `terrainDef.lattice` instead of its own `def`. That class of\n// bug is IMPOSSIBLE while ctx and the global are one binding. It only becomes possible when ctx\n// becomes a real snapshot, which is Phase B, by which time every read site has been migrated.\n//\n// ── HOW A PLUGIN GETS THE LATTICE ───────────────────────────────────────────\n// Three things, deliberately separated (ch26 = 26-hexagonal-lattice.md; note the brief's\n// \"26-hexagonal-grids.md\" does not exist):\n//   * PURE CONSTANTS, zero coupling — HEX_ROW(:6143), HEX_NB_EVEN/ODD(:6172-3), HEX_EX/HEX_EY(:6178).\n//     These are the only clean leaf in the area. They go in src/core/lattice-constants.ts and\n//     import NOTHING.\n//   * DOCUMENT STATE — terrainDef(:6133), RES(:972), SCALE_RES(:1219), BUILD_QUALITY(:1222),\n//     XF(:1109). src/core/document.ts. Imports nothing.\n//   * DERIVED PREDICATES — isHex, cellSizeM, latticeRows, fieldW, fieldH, fieldLen, newField,\n//     sampleBilinear. They read state, so they belong WITH field, not with the constants.\n//     src/core/field.ts imports both of the above.\n// Fix that direction once, up front: constants -> nothing, state -> nothing, derived -> both,\n// kernels -> derived. Otherwise field.ts and lattice.ts become mutually importing, because\n// latticeRows(:1064) reads terrainDef(:6133) — a 5,069-line forward reference that works today only\n// because nothing is CALLED until boot() at :8109.\n//\n// ctx exposes the two INLINED lattice idioms as primitives — parityShift(y) (the\n// `terrainDef.lattice===\"hex\"?0.5:0` at :1158,1173,1190,1196,1548,1980,2650,6768) and rowPitch\n// (the `isHex()?HEX_ROW:1` at :2650,2822,3488,4913,4940,6768,6929,7163,7370,7373) — but MIGRATING\n// a call site to them must hoist the value to exactly where the local const already sits. It must\n// NEVER re-associate the float expression: `(x+hxs*(y&1))/n` is not the same rounding as\n// `x/n+hxs*(y&1)/n`, and every one of these sits in a per-pixel loop the digest hashes.\n//\n// ── HOW A PLUGIN GETS AUXILIARY MAPS ────────────────────────────────────────\n// READ: `ports` is keyed by inPorts[].label and carries the raw Float32Array BY REFERENCE.\n// The physical type travels beside it in FIELD_META (index.html:3202), reached via ctx.meta(f):\n// {kind:'temperature', unit:'°C', temperatureC, solarShadow, solarExposure, lapseRate, heightField}.\n// Declared inPorts[].accepts is the ch27 wire name for what that port expects. In Phase A the\n// declaration is inert; the RUNTIME check stays exactly where it is today (temperatureCFromField()\n// returning null, node writes _inputError). Do not move that check in Phase A — 4 nodes' error\n// strings are user-visible and 2 of them are digested through their returned newField().\n// WRITE: `writes: Port[]` + the returned Outputs object. The runtime scatters, not the node.\n//\n// ── RNG: THERE ISN'T ONE, AND THERE MUST NOT BE ─────────────────────────────\n// ctx exposes `hash(x,y,seed)` — the exact 32-bit integer hash at index.html:1128, `Math.imul`\n// based so it is reproducible bit-for-bit in GLSL `uint`, which is what lets the GPU fast path\n// generate the same noise as the CPU reference. It is a PURE function, not a stream.\n// A stateful RNG would be a determinism bug, not a convenience: evalGraphProgressive\n// (index.html:4863-4891) interleaves node evaluation across requestAnimationFrame frames and can be\n// CANCELLED mid-cone (`if(run!==buildRun)return false` at :4870, :4876, :4887). Any shared mutable\n// stream would make output depend on frame scheduling and on how far a cancelled build got.\n// Every seed in this app is already a param (P.seed, index.html:4030, 11 declarations), which is\n// ch14-compliant. ctx.hash is the only randomness a plugin may touch.\n//\n// ── GRAPH RE-ENTRANCY: GRANTED, NOT AMBIENT ─────────────────────────────────\nexport function withResolution<T>(w:number, fn:()=>T): T {           // wraps index.html:2611\n  const prev = RES; RES = w; try { return fn() } finally { RES = prev }\n}\nexport function withTransform<T>(xf:Affine, fn:()=>T): T {           // wraps index.html:4263-4264\n  const prev = XF; XF = xfMul(xf, prev); try { return fn() } finally { XF = prev }\n}\n// evaluateUpstream(slot, {exact}) is granted ONLY to plugins declaring `reentrant: true`.\n// Exactly one plugin does: transform. It is the sole node that cannot be expressed as\n// (params, inputs, ctx) -> outputs, because what it does is \"re-run my upstream cone under a\n// different coordinate frame\". The capability wraps evalExact (index.html:4059-4066), which\n// deliberately does NOT write nd._field — the cached fields are the UNtransformed ones other\n// branches and the thumbnails depend on (comment at :4057-4058). Both of transform's paths are\n// pinned: the exact path by the default params, and the raster path by\n// _verify_digest.js:291-292, which forces mode:'raster' precisely because the default never\n// reaches transformField().\nCO-EVOLUTION: ENFORCEMENT IS TWO-SIDED. One check is a runtime invariant the shim cannot be talked out of; the\nother is a static ratchet that can only shrink. Neither can be satisfied by writing a comment.\n\nSIDE A — RUNTIME COMPLETENESS. `assertWritesExact(spec, out)` inside the generated eval throws if\nthe key set of the returned Outputs is not IDENTICAL to spec.writes.map(p => p.map). A declared map\nthat is not returned throws. A returned map that is not declared throws. Consequences:\n  * A node physically cannot forget a map it declared, and cannot smuggle one out undeclared.\n  * There is no longer anywhere to put a computed quantity except a declared port. That is exactly\n    the failure this repo already has, in the concrete: `let hydroMassDiag=null` (index.html:1443)\n    is a module-level, last-writer-wins global carrying hydraulic erosion's entire mass budget\n    (sumIn, sumOut, settled, exported, lost, brushClipGain), written by the pipe path at :1495 and\n    the droplet path at :3906. It exists because the current signature returns ONE Float32Array and\n    has nowhere else to put what the simulation computed. Two erosion nodes in one graph and the\n    first budget is destroyed before anything can read it. ch27 §Verification requires a closed\n    budget for every conserved quantity; this is that requirement failing in code, not in theory.\n  * ch27 §Verification's review test — \"a node that changed height without touching the maps its\n    process implies fails review by inspection; the output-port list is the tell\" — becomes a\n    machine-readable list instead of a reviewer's memory.\n\nSIDE B — THE STATIC ch27 RATCHET. A new gate, tests/legacy/_verify_coevolution.js, reads a\nmachine-readable transcription of ch27's co-evolution table (origin/main:terrain-architect/\nreferences/27-engine-data-handoff.md, §The First-Class Citizen Rule) and asserts, for every plugin\ndeclaring `process: P`:  writes ⊇ CH27_REQUIRES[P].\n    CH27_REQUIRES = {\n      hydraulic:        ['height','sedimentDepth','soilDepth','wetness','flowVelocity','waterDepth'],\n      thermal:          ['height','soilDepth'],\n      aeolian:          ['height','sandDepth','moisture'],\n      glacial:          ['height','soilDepth','wetness','flowVelocity'],\n      'soil-production':['soilDepth'],\n      orographic:       ['moisture'],\n      snow:             ['snowDepth','wetness','soilDepth'],\n    }\nPlugins with no `process` are exempt — a Blur is not a physical process.\n\nTHE RATCHET IS WHAT MAKES IT LAND GREEN. A checked-in tests/legacy/coevolution-debt.json records\ntoday's gaps, one entry per (nodeId, missingMap), each with a reason. The gate fails on:\n  (i)  any gap NOT in the debt file  -> a new node cannot ship non-compliant;\n  (ii) any entry in the debt file that is no longer a gap -> the file must shrink when you fix one.\nSo it passes on the first commit and can only move one direction. A plugin author adding a hydraulic\nnode must either co-update the maps or add a debt entry, which is visible in review as a named\nregression rather than an absence.\n\nTODAY'S DEBT, MEASURED FROM THE CODE (not copied from ch27's prose). I mapped every `nd._*` write\ninside TYPES (index.html:4068-4651) to a node id — 13 nodes, 13 distinct field names:\n  ALREADY ch27-COMPLIANT STATE PORTS (4 of the registry's ~14 maps exist):\n    temperature  — d_temperature/_temperatureC and d_heat/_temperatureC, in °C, span -100..1400\n                   (TEMP_MIN_C/TEMP_MAX_C, index.html:3202). ch27 unit: °C. Matches.\n    windVector   — d_wind/_wind and d_windmodify/_wind carry {u,v,speed} in m/s. ch27 demands\n                   world-space (u,v) m/s, RG32F, never normalised. Matches (speed is kept as data).\n    snowDepth    — snow/_snowLayer.depthM in metres, with a closed volume/deposited/melted/moved\n                   budget already digested as 7 scalars (_verify_digest.js:271-275). Matches, and it\n                   is the model for what every other state map should look like.\n    insolation   — d_sunshadow/_solarExposure. ch27: \"received fraction vs flat unshadowed ground\",\n                   explicitly distinct from ao. Matches. (_solarShadow is an implementation detail\n                   of that pass, not a registry map — declare it `kind:'preview'`, not state.)\n  ch27-COMPLIANT DERIVED MAPS (correctly recomputed from final height, ch27 §State vs derived):\n    d_slope, d_height, d_curvature (Zevenbergen-Thorne), d_occlusion (=ao), d_flow, d_deposits,\n    d_wear, d_texture. These are NOT state maps and must not be promoted to state maps.\n  IN DEBT — 6 nodes, the full list:\n    hydraulic     missing soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity, waterDepth, sedimentDepth\n    streampower   missing soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity, waterDepth, sedimentDepth\n    erosion2      missing soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity, waterDepth, sedimentDepth\n    hydrofix      missing soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity, waterDepth\n    thermal       missing soilDepth  (ch27: \"talus is loose debris — it ADDS to the cover it lands on\")\n    snow          missing wetness (meltwater), soilDepth (avalanche-track scour)\n  ABSENT FROM THE REGISTRY ENTIRELY, no producer node exists: moisture (ch27 calls it \"the absolute\n  gatekeeper\" — biome, vegetation, snow supply and the rainfall term of every erosion node all read\n  it), strataHardness, sedimentDepth, sandDepth, soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity, waterDepth.\n  Note ch27's Snow Rule (\"no moisture = no new snow\") is currently UNSATISFIABLE here, because no\n  node produces `moisture`. That is a Phase B/C node, not a Phase A refactor.\n\nONE NAMING CORRECTION THE CONTRACT MUST CARRY: this app's `weathering` node (index.html:4626) is a\nCOLOUR effect, not ch27's weathering/soil-production process (the Heimsath production function).\nIt must declare `process: undefined` and `writes:[{map:'albedo',kind:'preview'}]`, or the ratchet\nwill demand soilDepth from a node that paints rock stains.\n\nWHY THIS BELONGS IN PHASE A EVEN THOUGH NO MAP IS ADDED: the enforcement mechanism is\ndigest-neutral (assertWritesExact only observes declared-vs-returned keys; the ratchet reads the\nmanifest and never runs the app), while the thing it enforces — that a physical node's output-port\nlist is complete — is precisely the property Phase B's 60-node extraction will be tempted to\nviolate 60 times. Landing the gate before the extraction means every Phase B node is checked as it\narrives, rather than audited afterwards.\nDIGEST SAFETY: FIVE INDEPENDENT MECHANISMS. Each is a measured property of this repo, not an aspiration.\n\n1. THE ORACLE IS NEVER EDITED IN PHASE A — that is what the shim is for.\n   _verify_digest.js:189 does `def.eval(nd.params, ins, nd)` and requires a Float32Array back;\n   :190 `propagateFieldMetadata(f, ins, def)` reads def.fieldSemantics; :182 def.referenceOnly;\n   :154 def.params[].key/.def; :183 nodeInputs(nd) -> TYPES[nd.type].ins; :414 Object.keys(TYPES).\n   definePlugin() GENERATES an `eval` with exactly that signature and return type. The plugin author\n   writes compute(params, ports, ctx, node) -> Outputs; the registry emits the legacy arrow. So the\n   modern contract lands without a single byte of the oracle changing.\n   The alternative — changing eval's return to {height, aux} — has four call sites and one of them\n   IS the oracle (index.html:4845 evalGraph.ev, :4880 evalGraphProgressive, :4064 evalExact,\n   _verify_digest.js:189). Any commit that moves the app and the oracle together converts \"60/60\n   green\" from evidence into a tautology. The shim costs one closure call per node evaluation and\n   buys the entire migration. That is the single most important decision in this design.\n\n2. ADDITIVE MANIFEST FIELDS ARE UNOBSERVABLE — measured, not argued.\n   I grepped all 8,112 lines for Object.keys(def), Object.entries(def), `for (k in def)` and\n   JSON.stringify(def) over a TYPES entry: ZERO hits. The complete set of properties ever read off a\n   TYPES entry is 11 — cat, name, ins, desc, params, eval, note, info, fieldSemantics,\n   referenceOnly, passthrough — plus `effect`, declared on 7 nodes and read 0 times. Nothing\n   serialises a TYPES entry (graphSnapshot at index.html:4660-4665 copies node instances, never the\n   type). Therefore an unknown key on an entry cannot reach any code path. `writes`, `locality`,\n   `resolutionPolicy`, `process`, `inPorts`, `gpuVariant`, `exactUnderTransform` are free.\n   Keep it free: _verify_manifest.js should include a scan asserting no new enumeration of a TYPES\n   entry has appeared.\n\n3. THE CONTEXT ALIASES THE GLOBALS; IT DOES NOT SNAPSHOT THEM.\n   There are exactly 8 hidden inputs, and I confirmed each declaration: 4 mutable — RES(:972),\n   XF(:1109), SCALE_RES(:1219), terrainDef(:6133) — and 4 module consts that behave as hidden inputs\n   because they are never passed: HEX_SQUARE_WORLD(:1059), REF_RES(:1220), HEX_ROW(:6143),\n   BUILD_QUALITY(:1222). TWO of the four mutables are WRITTEN DURING EVALUATION: RES by\n   atFeatureScale(:2611) and XF by transform's own eval(:4263-4264).\n   Because CTX.res is `get res(){return RES}` over the same binding, a read through ctx and a read\n   of the global are the same read of the same storage, at every instant, including inside a\n   rebinding scope. There is no value that ctx can report differently from today. A snapshot could,\n   and would drift erosion2 immediately: erosionScale ships 0.38, featureScale=lerp(1,7,0.38)=3.28\n   (index.html:3924), 3.28>1.02, so atFeatureScale's RES rebind genuinely fires at the digest's 256².\n\n4. REFERENCE IDENTITY IS PRESERVED END TO END.\n   The shim passes ins[i] by reference into `ports` and returns out.height itself. No copy, no wrap,\n   no subarray. This protects four things the digest would not catch equally:\n     * FIELD_META (index.html:3202) is a WeakMap keyed on ARRAY IDENTITY. A copy drops every °C tag\n       and tempmask/d_heat/snow/water fall into their \"Connect a Temperature field\" branches\n       (:4461,:4505,:4592-4594) — 6+ digests move from what looks like plumbing. This one the digest\n       DOES catch, loudly, which is why it is worth stating.\n     * Four caches keyed by array reference identity: _colorErosionRoute (:5978,:5998),\n       _weatherSignal (:6031,:6060), _biomeMaskCache (:6094), and updateViewport's\n       curField/curSnowLayerRef (:6819-6822). These the digest does NOT catch: a fresh array per\n       call means they miss forever, a silent 5-50x regression that passes the gate cleanly.\n       Contract rule: identical key => identical array INSTANCE.\n     * The oracle's aux reach() (:276) finds nd._temperatureC / nd._wind.u / nd._snowLayer.depthM\n       only if the shim scatters the same objects under the same names.\n     * CANYON_EVOLUTION_CACHE (index.html:2088), the one genuinely content-addressed cache, keyed by\n       13 joined scalars including terrainDef.scale/height (:2130-2136), LRU-capped at 10 (:2499).\n       The oracle clears it per node type (:149). Its mutate-on-hit (:2253-2262 canyonComposeField\n       writes result.field into the shared entry) stays as-is in Phase A — it is deterministic under\n       the single-threaded loop and the digest pins it.\n\n5. FLOAT EXPRESSIONS ARE MOVED, NEVER RE-ASSOCIATED.\n   Extraction of the two inlined lattice idioms (the `terrainDef.lattice===\"hex\"?0.5:0` half-cell\n   shift at 8 sites — :1158,1173,1190,1196,1548,1980,2650,6768 — and the `isHex()?HEX_ROW:1` row\n   pitch at 10 sites — :2650,2822,3488,4913,4940,6768,6929,7163,7370,7373) must hoist the value to\n   exactly where the local const already sits. `(x+hxs*(y&1))/n` is not the same rounding as\n   `x/n+hxs*(y&1)/n` in IEEE754, and every one of these sits in a per-pixel loop the digest hashes.\n   warpField:2650 is the only site holding both idioms, and its comment at :2646-2659 records that\n   transposing du/dv there was a real ~15% anisotropy bug found by review and invisible to every gate.\n\nWHERE 60/60 GREEN IS NOT SUFFICIENT EVIDENCE — the digest's observation window, measured:\n  * CPU only. USE_GPU=false is pinned at _verify_digest.js:110, so the gpuReady() branch in 6 node\n    evals (perlin:4072 simplex:4076 ridged:4080 warp:4208 thermal:4298 hydraulic:4363, + erosion2 via\n    :3938) is NEVER exercised. Gate: _verify_gpu.js — and it IS in the frozen bridge surface.\n  * Square lattice only. terrainDef is reset at :114-115 without a `lattice` key, so it stays\n    \"square\". Every hex path is unobserved. Gates: _verify_hex*.js (5 files), _verify_flip.js,\n    _verify_wireframe.js — 8 of which are NOT in the frozen surface today (step 1 fixes that).\n  * RES 256 only. `Math.min(full,512)` collapses to 256, so preview===full and the 512-cap preview\n    pyramid in evaluateSunShadowMap(:3298), evaluateTemperatureMap(:3307), simulateTerrainWind(:3537)\n    and evaluateSnowLayer(:3789) never fires. That branch uses resampleField (:2580-2588), which is\n    SQUARE-ONLY (`new Float32Array(toN*toN)`) while a hex field is RES x round(RES*2/sqrt(3)) — 26\n    resampleField call sites vs 4 resampleRect. Needs a new gate at RES=1024 interactive on both\n    lattices BEFORE anyone refactors it. Export resampleRect as primary; keep resampleField as a\n    named square-in-square-out alias with the 26 sites listed as un-migrated remainder.\n  * Cold evaluation only. The oracle rebuilds the graph per node type and sets _dirty:true on every\n    node (:147-168), exercising zero of markDirtyFrom(:4770)/markParamDirty(:4779)/\n    applyWorkingResolution(:7655)/the terrainDef slider handlers(:5431-5445). The measured\n    under-invalidation (canyon reads terrainDef.scale/height in its cache key at :2131 but the canyon\n    NODE is never marked dirty on a terrainDef edit) is invisible to it.\n  * Acyclic only. evalGraph's ev() feeds a null input and caches with _dirty=false; graphEvalOrder()\n    drops the back-edge in `visiting`. Two different cycle semantics, neither pinned.\n  * No performance dimension at all.\nCUT LIST:\n  1. 0. A plan that reports only 'digest green' for those three is reporting the instrument, not the behaviour.\n\n### DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A\nCONTRACT:\n// ===========================================================================\n// src/core/plugin.ts — THE CONTRACT.\n//\n// The single design decision everything else follows from: definePlugin() is an\n// ADAPTER that RETURNS A LEGACY TYPES ENTRY. The new contract is the authoring\n// surface; the 12-key object at index.html:4068-4651 stays the runtime surface.\n// That is what makes 60/60 hold at every commit (see digestSafety).\n// ===========================================================================\n\n/** ch27 wire names ARE the port names — 27 §Naming: \"the tool never re-names per\n *  engine\". MEASURED: 8 of these occur ZERO times in the 603 KB index.html\n *  (soilDepth, sedimentDepth, sandDepth, strataHardness, moisture, wetness,\n *  insolation, flowVelocity). They are new vocabulary, not renames of anything. */\ntype FieldKind =\n  | 'height' | 'mask' | 'albedo'                                   // app-native\n  | 'moisture' | 'temperature' | 'insolation' | 'windVector'       // 27 climate\n  | 'soilDepth' | 'sedimentDepth' | 'sandDepth' | 'strataHardness' // 27 geology\n  | 'wetness' | 'flowVelocity' | 'waterDepth' | 'waterSurface'     // 27 hydrology\n  | 'snowDepth'\n  | 'curvature' | 'ao' | 'slope' | 'aspect';                       // 27 geometry\n\n/** 14:70-79 wants the unit IN THE DESCRIPTOR. Today it is a display string inside\n *  63 inline `fmt` closures and nothing reads it; `P.number` carries a real `unit`\n *  on only 5 of 235 param declarations. 'cells' is the ⚠ class of 14:85-86 and\n *  MECHANICALLY forces resolution:'bound' at registration. */\ntype Unit = 'm' | 'm2' | 'm3' | 'deg' | 'tan' | 'degC' | 'degC/km' | 'm/s'\n          | 'mm/yr' | '1/yr' | 'ratio' | 'norm01' | 'cells' | 'none';\n\ninterface PortDesc {\n  /** MUST reproduce the legacy `ins[]` label byte-for-byte and IN ORDER. The digest\n   *  wires a distinct perlin seed per slot (_verify_digest.js SEEDS/WIRING) exactly\n   *  so a slot swap moves the hash — port order is a hashed quantity, not cosmetic. */\n  label: string;\n  kind: FieldKind;\n  unit: Unit;\n  /** 1 = R32F, 2 = RG32F (windVector, flowVelocity — 27 §Vector encoding), 3 = RGB\n   *  (albedo). Today `_wind` is a 3-array object and colour is resolved in a whole\n   *  second graph walk; arity makes both a declared shape instead of a convention. */\n  arity: 1 | 2 | 3;\n  required?: boolean;\n  /** 27 §State maps vs derived maps — OPPOSITE lifecycle rules, and conflating them\n   *  is the defect the chapter exists to kill. 'state' may never be recomputed from\n   *  final geometry; 'derived' must be recomputed, never patched. */\n  lifecycle: 'state' | 'derived';\n  /** derived ports only: the ports this is a pure function of. Feeds 27's manifest\n   *  parent-hash rule (\"an engine that loads curvature whose parent hash does not\n   *  match height's is loading a lie\"). */\n  derivedFrom?: string[];\n  /** PHASE A ONLY. Where the adapter scatters this port onto the mutable node object\n   *  so _verify_digest.js's reach(nd,'_wind.u'), collectScene, resolveColor and the\n   *  viewport keep finding it unchanged. This field is the entire reason the aux\n   *  contract can land with the gate green. Deleted in Phase C, one node at a time,\n   *  each with its own oracle-accessor commit. */\n  legacySlot?: string;   // '_temperatureC' | '_wind.u' | '_snowLayer.depthM' | ...\n  /** referenceOnly:[0] today (drawmask, index.html:4096). Honoured by exactChain/\n   *  evalExact and by the digest's ev(), NOT by either in-app evaluator — drawmask\n   *  fakes it with `nd._reference=ins[0]`. Making it a port property puts the rule\n   *  in one place without changing who currently performs it. */\n  reference?: boolean;\n}\n\ninterface ParamDesc<T = unknown> {\n  key: string;                  // hashed: cloneParams(pr.def) keys the node's params\n  label: string;                // free to change — never hashed\n  type: 'slider' | 'number' | 'select' | 'seg' | 'tabs' | 'text' | 'curve' | 'seed';\n  unit: Unit;                   // 14:83-87, structural. ADDITIVE — nothing reads it yet.\n  def: T;                       // HASHED. Changing any default moves that node's digest.\n  min?: number; max?: number; step?: number;\n  scale?: 'log'; floor?: number; zero?: boolean;\n  opts?: Array<[string, string]> | null;   // null = resolve from a live registry (satmap)\n  when?: { key: string; values: string[] };// WHEN() — 20 uses / 8 nodes, pure data, safe\n  group?: string;                          // GROUP() — 40 uses / 5 nodes\n  /** 14 §Dirty propagation. VALUE reuses the plan; TOPOLOGY re-plans. COMPOSE_ONLY is\n   *  the generalisation of canyon's already-shipped split: canyonCacheKey (index.html\n   *  :2130-2136) hashes 9 of 11 params and EXCLUDES `depth` because depth is an affine\n   *  gain over the cached solve. That is the only content-addressed cache in the app\n   *  and it is the template — do not invent a new one. */\n  invalidates: 'VALUE' | 'TOPOLOGY' | 'COMPOSE_ONLY';\n  /** 14:86 — a `cells` unit is a DECLARED resolution-dependence and forces\n   *  resolution:'bound'. Measured candidates today: blur.radius, d_deposits.radius,\n   *  d_peaks.radius, sculpt.radius (all multiplied by resScale() at dispatch) and\n   *  hydraulic.radius, which literally formats itself as \" px\". */\n  fmt?: (v: T) => string;       // CODE. Kept, but see `serialisable` below.\n}\n\ninterface PluginSpec {\n  /** 14:23 — stable and VERSIONED: \"erosion.pipe/2\". The version is what makes an\n   *  algorithm change invalidate the content-addressed cache instead of silently\n   *  serving stale terrain. Legacy id (the TYPES key) stays separate because the\n   *  digest baseline and every saved graph key off it. */\n  typeId: string;               // 'erosion.hydraulic/1'\n  legacyId: string;             // 'hydraulic' — the TYPES key. FROZEN.\n  cat: 'gen' | 'comb' | 'filt' | 'ero' | 'mask' | 'data' | 'effect' | 'out';\n  name: string;\n  desc: string;                 // 6,732 chars across 60 entries, zero markup. Data.\n  note?: string;                // 22 of 60. Data.\n  info?: (nd: NodeInstance) => string;  // 1 of 60 (transform). CODE — see `serialisable`.\n\n  inPorts: PortDesc[];\n  outPorts: PortDesc[];         // outPorts[0] IS the legacy return value.\n  params: ParamDesc[];\n\n  // ---- 14:26-29 flags, all currently UNDECLARED in a 60-node library ------------\n  /** The tiling contract (14:47-51). \"Flow accumulation and stream power are GLOBAL —\n   *  they cannot be tiled, full stop.\" d_flow, streampower, water's priorityFloodFill/\n   *  d8Accumulation and histeq are GLOBAL today and nothing says so. */\n  locality: 'local' | { neighbourhood: { metres: number } } | 'global';\n  /** The preview contract (14:53-57). hydraulic with a fixed droplet count is BOUND;\n   *  world-unit noise is INVARIANT. */\n  resolution: 'invariant' | 'bound';\n  /** 14:112-115 — a BOUND node without a scaling policy previews with a \"preview\n   *  differs\" badge. resScale() (index.html:1221) IS this policy, ambient and\n   *  undeclared, called at 18 sites. Declaring it does not move it yet. */\n  scalingPolicy?: (ctx: EvalContext) => number;\n  determinism: 'deterministic' | { stochastic: { seedParam: string } };\n  device: 'cpu' | 'gpu' | 'either';\n  /** 6 evals call gpuReady() (perlin, simplex, ridged, warp, thermal, hydraulic) and\n   *  gpuReady is `USE_GPU && terrainDef.lattice!==\"hex\" && GPU.init()`. So \"GPU\n   *  variant, square lattice only\" is a real capability the code already branches on\n   *  inline. _verify_digest.js pins USE_GPU=false — the GPU branch is UNGATED by\n   *  60/60 and needs _verify_gpu.js run alongside. */\n  gpuLattices?: Array<'square'>;\n\n  // ---- ch27 co-evolution, the part that must be enforced -----------------------\n  /** The physical process class. Keys into CO_EVOLUTION_OBLIGATIONS, transcribed\n   *  verbatim from 27's node table. */\n  process?: 'hydraulic' | 'thermal' | 'aeolian' | 'glacial' | 'weathering'\n          | 'orographic' | 'snow' | 'none';\n  writes: FieldKind[];          // which STATE it mutates. [] for a pure analysis node.\n  /** Must satisfy: OBLIGATIONS[process] ⊆ outPorts.kind ∪ keys(coEvolutionDebt).\n   *  Checked at registration — throws, app does not boot. */\n  coEvolutionDebt?: Partial<Record<FieldKind, string>>;  // port -> WHY it is unmet\n\n  // ---- capabilities: the two nodes that cannot be pure -------------------------\n  /** transform (index.html:4258-4269) composes XF and RE-ENTERS the evaluator on its\n   *  own upstream cone. No (params,inputs,ctx)->outputs signature expresses that, so\n   *  it is a declared capability, not a hack. EXACT_TYPES (the hand-maintained\n   *  18-string Set at :4048) becomes `exactUnderTransform:true` on those 18 plugins\n   *  and the Set is COMPUTED from the registry — otherwise Phase B leaves a hardcoded\n   *  type-name list inside the core, which defeats plugin-per-node outright. */\n  exactUnderTransform?: boolean;\n  needsUpstreamReeval?: boolean;         // transform only. Grants ctx.evaluateUpstream.\n  /** The 5 \"effect\" nodes (satmap, colorerosion, weathering, satmapblend, colormixer)\n   *  do their real work in resolveColor (:6101-6128), a SECOND graph walk with its own\n   *  memo. `effect:\"<id>\"` is already declared on 7 nodes (:4567-4645) and READ\n   *  NOWHERE — zero occurrences of `.effect` in 8,112 lines. Promoting it to the real\n   *  discriminator replaces two hardcoded `nd.type===` chains with dispatch, and this\n   *  function is the declared albedo output port. */\n  effect?: string;\n  resolveAlbedo?: (a: AlbedoArgs) => Float32Array | null;\n\n  // ---- instance state: `def.params` is NOT the whole node state, for 2 of 60 -----\n  /** colormixer: 2-15 runtime slots, ins overridden per-instance via nd._inputs, plus\n   *  params.layers. drawmask: params.strokes. Both are hardcoded in makeNode\n   *  (:4722-4727) AND special-cased in the oracle (_verify_digest.js mk()). Declaring\n   *  them is the difference between a contract with two escape hatches and a contract\n   *  that is simply false for 2 of 60. */\n  variadicPorts?: { min: number; max: number; template: PortDesc; layerParam: string };\n  instanceState?: Record<string, () => unknown>;   // 'strokes' -> () => []\n\n  /** propagateFieldMetadata (:3225-3235) — 5 of 60 declare it, default is DROP, and\n   *  the default is deliberate (comment at :3222-3224). A migration that defaults to\n   *  'preserve' changes 55 node types at once. Kept as an explicit tri-state. */\n  fieldSemantics?: 'preserve-primary' | 'temperature-pair' | 'drop';\n  /** markParamDirty (:4781) reads passthrough for 7 types; the evaluators carry a\n   *  SECOND, inconsistent rule (`nd.type!==\"water\"&&nd.type!==\"snow\"`, :4852/:4886).\n   *  Two facets of one concept, disagreeing by construction. The contract carries\n   *  both separately so unifying them is a decision, not an accident. */\n  paramsAffectHeight: boolean;   // false => dirty this node only\n  paramsAffectColour: boolean;   // false => do not set colorDirty\n\n  /** TYPES is CODE, not data: 63 inline fmt closures (13 close over terrainDef, which\n   *  is declared 2,065 lines LATER), one default that is a function CALL at module\n   *  init (mountain skirt), one `opts:null`, one `info(nd)` that reads an evaluation\n   *  side effect (nd._xfMode). A manifest claiming to be declarative must SAY which\n   *  half it is. `false` is legal and common; it just means \"not preset-serialisable\n   *  yet\" and a gate can count it down. */\n  serialisable: boolean;\n\n  /** THE ONE ENTRY POINT. All ports, one call, one intermediate state. There is no\n   *  postProcess, no second pass, no way to write an aux map from a later walk — that\n   *  structural absence IS 27's \"in the same pass, from the same intermediate state\".\n   *  Returns a record keyed by outPorts[].label; a `budget` per conserved quantity\n   *  replaces the module-level last-writer-wins hydroMassDiag (:1443/:1495/:3906). */\n  evaluate(a: EvalArgs): {\n    ports: Record<string, Float32Array>;\n    budget?: Record<string, MassBudget>;\n    diagnostic?: Record<string, number | string>;\n  };\n}\n\ninterface EvalArgs { params: Record<string, unknown>; inputs: (Float32Array|null)[];\n                     ctx: EvalContext; node: NodeInstance; }\ninterface MassBudget { in: number; out: number; settled: number;\n                       exported: number; lost: number; named?: Record<string, number>; }\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// THE ADAPTER. This function is the whole digest-safety argument.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndeclare function definePlugin(spec: PluginSpec): LegacyTypeEntry;\n\ninterface LegacyTypeEntry {          // byte-compatible with TYPES[k] today\n  cat: string; name: string; desc: string;\n  ins: string[];                     // = spec.inPorts.map(p => p.label), same order\n  params: ParamDesc[];               // = spec.params, same order, same `def` values\n  /** A PLAIN, WRITABLE, OWN DATA PROPERTY. Not a getter, not frozen, not a Proxy.\n   *  _verify_build_progress.js:21 does `TYPES.blur.eval = (...args)=>...realBlur(...)`\n   *  and bridge-surface.json records TYPES as `patched`. A getter or Object.freeze\n   *  breaks that gate silently. Signature and return type unchanged:\n   *      (p, ins, nd) => Float32Array of length fieldLen()\n   *  Body: build ctx -> spec.evaluate -> scatter ports[i>0] to their legacySlot on nd\n   *        -> return ports[outPorts[0].label]  (THE SAME INSTANCE — see risks/FIELD_META)\n   */\n  eval: (p: any, ins: (Float32Array|null)[], nd: any) => Float32Array;\n  note?: string; info?: (nd: any) => string;\n  passthrough?: boolean;             // = !spec.paramsAffectHeight\n  effect?: string;                   // now READ, by resolveColor's dispatcher\n  fieldSemantics?: string;\n  referenceOnly?: number[];          // = indices of inPorts where reference === true\n}\nEVAL CONTEXT:\nEvalContext replaces the EIGHT ambient hidden inputs the compute path reads today. Measured inside the TYPES literal alone (index.html:4068-4651): gpuReady() 7 sites, terrainDef 15, resScale() 6, XF 3, BUILD_QUALITY 3, fieldLen() 2, cellSizeM() 1, atFeatureScale 2, newField( 51. Four are MUTABLE globals — RES (:972), terrainDef (:6133), SCALE_RES (:1219), XF (:1109) — and two of those four are WRITTEN DURING EVALUATION: RES by atFeatureScale (:2611, `RES=w;try{out=fn(small);}finally{RES=prev;}  // the kernels read the global RES`) and XF by transform's own eval (:4263-4264).\n\nTHE ONE NON-OBVIOUS RULE, and it is the difference between green and red:\n\n**In Phase A, EvalContext is a LIVE VIEW, not a snapshot.** Every resolution- or document-derived field is an accessor over the existing module binding, never a copied value:\n\n    const ctx = {\n      get res()      { return RES; },              // NOT `res: RES`\n      get rows()     { return latticeRows(RES); },\n      get lattice()  { return terrainDef.lattice; },\n      get cellSizeM(){ return terrainDef.scale / RES; },\n      get resScale() { return SCALE_RES ? RES / REF_RES : 1; },\n      get quality()  { return BUILD_QUALITY; },\n      get xf()       { return XF; },\n      get doc()      { return terrainDef; },       // scale, height, baseElevation,\n                                                   // latitude, north, seaTemp, lapseRate,\n                                                   // solarElevation, windDirection, windSpeed\n      device: 'cpu' | 'gpu',\n    };\n\nA frozen `{res: RES}` would make thermal, hydraulic and erosion2 run at full RES instead of RES/k inside atFeatureScale, and would make every generator under transform sample the wrong coordinate frame. That is a same-commit digest drift on thermal, streampower, hydraulic, erosion2 and transform. erosion2 is not hypothetical at the gate's own resolution: it ships erosionScale 0.38, so featureScale = lerp(1,7,0.38) = 3.28 > 1.02, and the RES-mutation path DOES execute at 256². Live accessors make the rebinding propagate exactly as the bare global read does — byte-identical by construction. Phase B replaces the accessors with real values only after the kernels take dimensions as parameters, and re-baselines nothing because by then nothing reads the global.\n\nWHAT THE CONTEXT CARRIES\n\n1. Lattice (26). `ctx.lattice.kind` 'square'|'hex'; `parityShift(y)` = the `terrainDef.lattice===\"hex\"?0.5:0` idiom, inlined 8 times today; `rowPitch()` = the `isHex()?HEX_ROW:1` idiom, inlined 10 times; `neighbours(y)` = HEX_NB_EVEN/HEX_NB_ODD/hexNb (:6172-6174), the D6 odd-r one-ring; `gradientBasis()` = HEX_EX/HEX_EY (:6178, sum(e_k e_k^T)=3I). Critically, 26 separates TWO coordinate systems and the contract must carry both, because conflating them measured r=0.574 against the square build: `domainUV(x,y)` (authored — u=(x+0.5(y&1))/W, v=y/H, no row compression) and `worldXY(x,y)` (physical — metres, y=r·s·√3/2). Generators use domain; kernels use world. HEX_ROW/HEX_NB_*/HEX_EX/HEX_EY are the only zero-coupling leaf in the file and become lattice-constants.ts with no imports at all.\n\n2. Field factory. `ctx.newField()` stays NULLARY. newField is the most-called symbol in the app (104 sites) and its return LENGTH is a function of two mutable globals; the digest is length-sensitive, so a signature change to newField(w,h) touches 104 sites in one commit and a single missed site returns a short array and drifts that node. Also `newVectorField()` for arity-2 ports.\n\n3. Auxiliary maps travel on TYPED PORTS, not through the context. This is deliberate and it is the ch27 point: `ctx.aux.get('soilDepth')` would be a second, untracked channel — exactly the shape the chapter calls \"appended hacks\". A node that needs soilDepth declares an inPort of kind 'soilDepth', and the graph either supplies it or the node does not run. The only aux the context exposes is `ctx.recordBudget(name, MassBudget)`, which the adapter stores PER NODE INSTANCE — killing hydroMassDiag's last-writer-wins failure (two erosion nodes in one graph destroy the first one's ledger today).\n\n4. Seeded RNG. `ctx.seed(paramSeed, salt?) -> uint32` and `ctx.rng(salt) -> () => float`. MEASURED GOOD NEWS: zero `Math.random` calls exist in the entire compute range 1019-4667 — both occurrences in the file are UI (randomSeed at :4716, the satmap shuffle at :5525). Nodes already hash their `seed` param, so 14:88-91's \"no node ever calls a global RNG\" is already satisfied structurally. What is missing is the DERIVATION `hash(rootSeed, nodeId, seedOffset)`. Introducing rootSeed would change the effective seed of all 11 seeded nodes and move their digests, so Phase A ships `ctx.seed = (paramSeed) => paramSeed` — an identity derivation, declared as a deliberate Phase-C deferral with a named re-baseline, not an oversight.\n\n5. Re-entrancy capabilities, granted only to plugins that declare them:\n   - `ctx.withResolution(w, fn)` — the named seam for atFeatureScale's RES rebinding. Same mutation, one place instead of ambient-everywhere.\n   - `ctx.withTransform(xf, fn)` — the named seam for transform's XF composition.\n   - `ctx.evaluateUpstream(slot, {xf})` — granted only when `needsUpstreamReeval`. Wraps exactChain/evalExact (:4051-4066). This is the ONE capability without which transform cannot be a plugin at all.\n\n6. `ctx.device` is EXCLUDED from the cache key (14:157-160) — which is a promise that CPU and GPU agree, enforceable only by _verify_gpu.js, because the digest pins USE_GPU=false and therefore gates the CPU branch only.\nCO-EVOLUTION: 27's co-evolution rule is unenforceable as written against this codebase, because a hard assertion would fail at least 6 of the 60 nodes at module-init and the app would not boot. So enforcement is SIX LAYERS, of which the first is structural and the third is a ratchet.\n\nMEASURED STARTING POINT. Of 27's 15 registry map names, EIGHT occur zero times in the entire 603,669-byte index.html: soilDepth, sedimentDepth, sandDepth, strataHardness, moisture, wetness, insolation, flowVelocity. What DOES exist, as ad-hoc node mutations rather than ports: temperature (`_temperatureC`, real °C, plus a FIELD_META unit tag), insolation-in-all-but-name (`_solarExposure`), windVector (`_wind.{u,v,speed}`, an RG pair), snowDepth (`_snowLayer.depthM` in metres with a closed volume/deposited/melted/moved budget). 13 of 60 evals write 13 distinct `_`-prefixed aux fields. The derived side is genuinely compliant already: d_slope, d_height, d_curvature, d_flow, d_occlusion, d_deposits, d_wear, d_peaks, d_texture and d_sunshadow are recomputed from final height, which is what 27 requires of derived maps — they must not be mistaken for state.\n\nLAYER 1 — STRUCTURAL, NOT A CHECK. `evaluate` is the single entry point and returns ALL ports from ONE call. There is no postProcess hook, no second walk, no way to write an aux map from a later pass. A node physically cannot compute soilDepth from a re-read of the final height, because it never sees the final height — it returns it. That is 27's \"in the same pass, from the same intermediate state\" made impossible to violate, and it is precisely why the return type is `Record<PortName, Field>` and not `Field` + node mutation. It is also the direct fix for the concrete failure: hydraulicErode computes a complete mass budget (sumIn, sumOut, settled, exported, lost, brushClipGain) and has literally nowhere to put it, so it goes to a module-level last-writer-wins global (`hydroMassDiag`, :1443, written at :1495 and :3906) with no node identity — two erosion nodes in one graph and the first ledger is destroyed.\n\nLAYER 2 — REGISTRATION-TIME WALL (throws; the app does not boot). `CO_EVOLUTION_OBLIGATIONS` is 27's node table transcribed as DATA, keyed by `process`:\n  hydraulic  -> soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity, waterDepth  (+ writes sedimentDepth)\n  thermal    -> soilDepth                                     (talus ADDS to the cover it lands on)\n  aeolian    -> moisture (gating), snowDepth when the medium is snow  (+ writes sandDepth)\n  glacial    -> soilDepth, wetness, flowVelocity\n  weathering -> soilDepth                                     (Heimsath production IS its producer)\n  orographic -> moisture\n  snow       -> wetness, soilDepth\ndefinePlugin asserts `OBLIGATIONS[process] ⊆ outPorts.kind ∪ keys(coEvolutionDebt)` and throws otherwise. For the 61st node written by a third party this is an absolute wall: declare `process:'hydraulic'`, and you either produce the four maps or you write down in a string why you do not.\n\nLAYER 3 — THE DEBT RATCHET (the honest part). `coEvolutionDebt: { soilDepth: 'no soil layer exists in this build' }`. A new gate `_verify_coevolution.js` sums the ledger and diffs against `_coevolution_baseline.json`. **Debt may shrink, never grow.** Today's baseline is measured and non-zero: hydraulic, thermal, streampower, erosion2 and hydrofix all owe soilDepth; hydraulic additionally owes wetness, flowVelocity and waterDepth; snow owes wetness and soilDepth; and the whole app owes `moisture`, which does not exist anywhere, so 27's Snow Rule (`snowfall = moisture · freezeFraction(temperature)`) is currently unenforceable BY MEASUREMENT rather than by opinion — the snow node's declared inputs are height/temperature/wind, with no moisture port to gate on. That converts an aspiration into a single monotone number a reviewer can read.\n\nLAYER 4 — BUDGET CLOSURE, PER INSTANCE. `evaluate` may return `budget` per conserved quantity; the adapter records it on the node instance. This is not new machinery — it is repointing machinery that already works. `_verify_erosion_mass.js` reads hydroMassDiag today (at :83-84) and is a real, red-first-authored gate with documented pre-fix numbers (GPU deficit 0.9193 → ~0.35; the CPU identity `sumIn - sumOut = exported + lost - brushClipGain` closes exactly). `_verify_hex.js` reads it too. So 27's \"Co-evolution budget\" verification hook EXISTS; it is just global and last-writer-wins. Making `budget` a declared output and repointing both gates in the same commit generalises it to every node with a conserved quantity, and snow's 7 digested scalars prove the aux contract must admit scalars as well as maps.\n\nLAYER 5 — LIFECYCLE, ASSERTED. Every port declares `lifecycle:'state'|'derived'`. A plugin whose only inPort kind is 'height' and whose `writes` is empty may not emit a `state` port — that shape is definitionally \"recomputed from final geometry\", which 27 forbids for state maps. Derived ports carry `derivedFrom` and inherit the parent's cache key, which is 27's manifest parent-hash rule made checkable (\"an engine that loads curvature whose parent hash does not match height's is loading a lie\").\n\nLAYER 6 — TYPED PORTS DO THE REST. Because aux maps travel on typed ports and NOT through the context, a node cannot quietly reach for soilDepth: it declares an inPort of kind 'soilDepth' and the graph supplies it or the node does not run. The embryo already exists and shows both the value and the trap — FIELD_META (:3202) is a WeakMap sidecar keyed by array identity, with only 5 of 60 nodes declaring `fieldSemantics`, and its default is DROP. Promoting it to `PortDesc.kind` is the right move; defaulting it to 'preserve' during migration would change 55 node types at once.\n\nWHAT VIOLATES THE CONTRACT TODAY, AND THE COST OF EACH\n- transform (1): BLOCKED outright — re-enters the evaluator under a mutated global XF. Cost: `needsUpstreamReeval` + `ctx.evaluateUpstream` + moving EXACT_TYPES' 18 strings into manifest flags. Both digest paths are pinned (the exact path is the default; EXERCISE forces `mode:'raster'`), so the fix is gated on both sides.\n- satmap, colorerosion, weathering, satmapblend, colormixer (5): real output is colour, produced by a second graph walk. Cost: `effect` promoted from an inert key (declared 7×, read 0×) to the dispatcher, plus `resolveAlbedo` as a declared port. Behaviour-identical; the digest folds resolveColor's RGB into all five.\n- hydraulic, thermal, streampower, erosion2, hydrofix (5): co-evolution debt as above. Cost: debt strings in Phase A; real soil/wetness/flow ports in Phase C, each moving that node's digest and needing its own `--write`.\n- d_sunshadow, d_temperature, d_heat, d_wind, d_windmodify, snow, satmap (7): aux is the real output, carried as node mutation. Cost: ZERO in Phase A — `legacySlot` reproduces the mutation exactly. This is the cheapest real win available.\n- colormixer, drawmask (2): `def.params` is not the whole node state; colormixer's port count is instance state. Cost: `variadicPorts` + `instanceState`, i.e. two declared escape hatches from day one rather than two undeclared ones.\n- ~36 nodes with `(p,ins)` signatures and no ambient reads beyond newField/maskApply are already compliant and are the cheap 60% of Phase B.\nDIGEST SAFETY: MECHANICALLY, the digest cannot move because the adapter makes the runtime surface structurally identical. I measured what the oracle actually observes, from the source (tests/legacy/_verify_digest.js, 594 lines), and the surface is small and closed:\n\nWHAT THE ORACLE READS\n1. `TYPES[type]` with `.params` (each `{key, def}` — cloneParams(pr.def) at mk():152-165), `.ins` via nodeInputs, `.referenceOnly`, `.name`, and `.eval`.\n2. `def.eval(nd.params, ins, nd)` called from its OWN private copy of ev() (:174-194) — the oracle does not call evalGraph.\n3. `nd.<dotted path>` after eval: AUX (:259-267, 7 node types) and SCALARS (:271-275, 7 float64s on snow).\n4. `resolveColor(nd.id, RES, {}, new Set())` for the 5 COLOUR types (:278).\n5. `priorityFloodFill` / `d8Accumulation` for water.\n6. The globals it ASSIGNS: RES, TARGET_RES, USE_GPU=false, SCALE_RES=true, BUILD_QUALITY='interactive', XF=null, terrainDef (Object.assign, leaving lattice='square'), H_SCALE, buildRun, evalFrame, nodes, edges, uid, selected, selectedEdge, CANYON_EVOLUTION_CACHE.\n\nTHREE HARD ASSERTIONS I VERIFIED IN THE GATE LOGIC, which constrain the design absolutely:\n- `if (primary.length !== RES*RES) → skipped` (:337). A `skipped` type becomes `missing` at the gate (:552-560) and `missing.length` FAILS (:583). So an eval that returns anything other than a Float32Array of length RES² does not degrade — it fails. This is why the eval return shape CANNOT change at the registry level in Phase A.\n- `added.length` also FAILS (:562-567). Adding a 61st node type goes RED until a deliberate `--write`. Phase A must add ZERO node types.\n- The oracle's ev() calls `propagateFieldMetadata(f, ins, def)` on the return value and assigns `nd._field = f`. Returning `{ports:{...}}` would tag and cache an object. Confirmed: the adapter is not a convenience, it is load-bearing.\n\nTHE FIVE MECHANISMS\n1. **definePlugin returns a plain object literal with the same 12 keys.** `ins` is `inPorts.map(p=>p.label)` in order; `params` is the same array with the same `def` values; `eval` is a plain writable own data property (required — _verify_build_progress.js:21 monkey-patches `TYPES.blur.eval`, and bridge-surface.json records TYPES as `patched`). The oracle cannot tell a synthesised entry from a hand-written one.\n2. **Phase A rewrites no eval body.** Steps A5-A13 add manifest fields that nothing reads at eval time, rename nothing that is hashed, and move code without re-associating a single float expression. The rule is explicit: hoist an idiom to the same lexical position the local const already occupies — `(x+hxs*(y&1))/n` is not the same rounding as `x/n+hxs*(y&1)/n`, and warpField:2650's comment documents a real ~15% anisotropy bug in this exact family that every gate missed.\n3. **The context is a live view**, so the two write-during-eval globals (RES at :2611, XF at :4263) still propagate into every kernel. A frozen context is the one change that would drift thermal/hydraulic/erosion2/transform in a single commit.\n4. **Aux maps land where the oracle already looks.** `legacySlot` makes the adapter assign `nd._temperatureC`, `nd._wind.u`, `nd._snowLayer.depthM` exactly as the 13 impure evals do today. `reach(nd, '_wind.u')` resolves identically. This is what lets a declared multi-map output contract exist with zero baseline change.\n5. **The primary buffer is returned BY IDENTITY.** FIELD_META (:3202) is a WeakMap keyed on the Float32Array OBJECT. The adapter must return `ports[outPorts[0].label]` itself — never a copy, wrapper, subarray or transferable — or every temperature tag is dropped and tempmask/d_heat/snow/water fall into their `_inputError` branches, moving 6+ digests from a change that reads as pure plumbing.\n\nWHAT 60/60 DOES **NOT** PROVE — measured, so the cut list names substitute gates\nThe oracle observes exactly one configuration: CPU (USE_GPU=false, :110), SQUARE (terrainDef Object.assign at :114-115 never sets lattice, and the RES² length assertion structurally forbids hex), RES=256 with SCALE_RES=true and BUILD_QUALITY='interactive'. Consequences:\n- `Math.min(full,512)` collapses to preview===full, so the 512-cap preview pyramid in evaluateSunShadowMap/evaluateTemperatureMap/simulateTerrainWind/evaluateSnowLayer NEVER executes. That branch calls resampleField (:2580-2588), which allocates `new Float32Array(toN*toN)` — square-only — across 25 sites versus 3 for the rect-correct resampleRect (:2594-2602). Latent on square, wrong on hex, and completely invisible to the gate.\n- The GPU branch of 6 node evals is ungated. _verify_gpu.js is the substitute.\n- Invalidation is ungated: the oracle tears down and rebuilds per node type with `_dirty:true` everywhere, so markDirtyFrom/markParamDirty/applyWorkingResolution and the terrainDef handlers are never exercised. The measured under-invalidation (canyon hashes terrainDef.scale/height in its cache key but the canyon NODE is never dirtied on a scale edit) is invisible.\n- Cache-hit performance is ungated. Four caches key on Float32Array REFERENCE IDENTITY (_colorErosionRoute, _weatherSignal, _biomeMaskCache, and the viewport's curField/curSnowLayerRef). A purified evaluator returning fresh arrays for unchanged inputs makes all four miss forever — a silent 5-50x regression the digest passes cleanly. Contract rule: identical key ⇒ identical array INSTANCE.\n- All digest graphs are acyclic; evalGraph.ev() (feeds null, caches with _dirty=false) and graphEvalOrder() (drops the back-edge in `visiting`) resolve cycles DIFFERENTLY and neither is pinned.\n\nEmpirically confirmed at the start of this design: `node tests/legacy/_verify_digest.js` prints \"covered 60 node types, skipped 0 of 60 in TYPES / PASS 60 node types bit-identical to the baseline at 256²\", exit 0.\nCUT LIST:\n  1.
