Phase A ships an identity derivation; a contract that quietly claimed per-node seeded RNG while shipping identity would be the same category of lie as the inert `effect` key.\n\n### DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile)\nCONTRACT:\n// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// src/core/plugin.ts — the Terrain Studio node plugin contract.\n// Every field is justified by a MEASURED fact in apps/terrain-studio/index.html, by the\n// oracle apps/terrain-studio/tests/legacy/_verify_digest.js, or by references/14, 26, 27.\n// The governing design constraint: `definePlugin()` returns an object that IS a superset of\n// today's TYPES entry, so `TYPES[t].eval(nd.params, ins, nd)` keeps existing unchanged and\n// the oracle never has to be edited to keep 60/60. See `digestSafety`.\n// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ntype Lattice = 'square' | 'hex'          // terrainDef.lattice — index.html:6134\ntype Quality = 'interactive' | 'final'   // BUILD_QUALITY      — index.html:1222\n\n/* ── ch27 Standard Map Registry. These are the WIRE NAMES (ch27 \"Naming\"); no per-engine\n      renaming, ever. Split by ch27's \"State maps vs derived maps\", which have OPPOSITE\n      lifecycles and therefore must be different types, not a flag on one type.            */\ntype StateMap   = 'height' | 'soilDepth' | 'sedimentDepth' | 'sandDepth' | 'snowDepth'\n                | 'wetness' | 'moisture' | 'waterDepth' | 'strataHardness'\ntype VectorMap  = 'windVector' | 'flowVelocity'          // ch27: paired R32F (RG32F)\ntype DerivedMap = 'curvature' | 'ao' | 'slope' | 'aspect' | 'insolation' | 'temperature'\n                | 'flowAccum' | 'waterSurface'\ntype AuxName    = StateMap | VectorMap | DerivedMap\n\n/* ── An auxiliary output PORT. This type is the whole ch27 retrofit, and it is the reason\n      Phase A must define it now: 13 of 60 evals already write 17 ad-hoc `nd._*` fields\n      (drawmask:4097 … satmapblend:4642) and the oracle already digests 7 of them by dotted\n      path (_verify_digest.js:259-275). Declaring them costs nothing; discovering them later\n      means touching all 60 plugins twice.                                                  */\ninterface AuxPort {\n  name: AuxName\n  kind: 'state' | 'derived'\n  //  ch27: state maps are path-dependent and may NEVER be recomputed from final geometry;\n  //  derived maps are pure functions of final fields and must be RECOMPUTED, never patched.\n  //  The runtime enforces the difference (see `coEvolution`), so it cannot be a comment.\n  unit: 'm' | 'm/s' | '°C' | 'ratio' | '1/m' | 'mm/yr' | '1/yr' | 'none'\n  //  ch14:83-87 \"the unit lives in the descriptor\". Today units exist only inside 63 inline\n  //  `fmt` closures (18 render metres, 7 render °C) — display strings, not data.\n  channels: 1 | 2\n  //  ch27 \"Vector encoding\": windVector/flowVelocity ship world-space (u,v) m/s, +X east,\n  //  +Y north, never normalised. Present today as `_wind.{u,v,speed}` (index.html:4487).\n  encoding?: 'linear' | { kind: 'temperature'; minC: number; maxC: number }\n  //  index.html:3202-3213 already encodes Celsius into [0,1] with TEMP_MIN_C=-100 /\n  //  TEMP_MAX_C=1400 and carries the meaning in a WeakMap sidecar. Promote sidecar → port.\n  legacySlot?: string\n  //  ★ THE DIGEST PIN. '_temperatureC', '_wind.u', '_snowLayer.depthM', '_driver'.\n  //  _verify_digest.js:276 `reach(nd, dotted)` reads aux off the NODE object. The generated\n  //  legacy `eval` shim scatters `Outputs.aux[name]` to this exact path, so the oracle finds\n  //  every byte where it looks today and 60/60 holds with ZERO oracle edits.\n}\n\n/* ── Bookkeeping that is not a field. Required because `snow` returns a struct with SEVEN\n      float64 scalars that the oracle pins individually (_verify_digest.js:271-275:\n      volumeM3, depositedM3, meltedM3, movedM3, maxDepthM, coverage, iterationsRun).\n      An aux model admitting only Float32Array forces snow back into a node mutation.       */\ninterface ScalarPort { name: string; unit: string; legacySlot?: string }\n\n/* ── ch27 \"Verification / Co-evolution budget\": total solid mass is invariant or the leak is\n      NAMED and MEASURED. This is not aspirational — the shape below is literally today's\n      `hydroMassDiag` (index.html:3906), a module-level last-writer-wins global that exists\n      only because eval returns one Float32Array and has nowhere else to put the budget.\n      _verify_erosion_mass.js gates it already (G1-G5c) and reads it BY NAME.               */\ninterface MassBudget {\n  process: ProcessId\n  stack: AuxName[]                    // ch27: sum the STACK, not the layers separately —\n                                      // layers legitimately exchange mass.\n  sumIn: number; sumOut: number\n  moved: number; exported: number; lost: number; gain: number\n  derived?: boolean                   // GPU pipes derive `exported` by elimination; the\n                                      // ledger must say so (_verify_erosion_mass.js G3).\n}\n\n/* ── The single return bundle. There is exactly ONE way for a node to emit anything.       */\ninterface Outputs {\n  height?: Float32Array   // MUST be ctx.newField()-shaped: length === ctx.len. The oracle\n                          // hard-asserts primary.length === RES*RES (_verify_digest.js:354)\n                          // and SKIPS the node otherwise, which reads as `missing` → FAIL.\n  aux?: Partial<Record<AuxName, Float32Array>>\n  scalars?: Record<string, number>\n  color?: Float32Array | null     // RGB, 3*len. Makes colour a DECLARED SECOND OUTPUT PORT\n                                  // instead of resolveColor's separate walk (index.html:6101)\n                                  // which today is the real output of 5 of 60 node types.\n  meta?: FieldMeta | null         // the FIELD_META tag (index.html:3202), explicit not implicit\n  budget?: MassBudget[]\n  error?: string | null           // → nd._inputError (index.html:4461, 4505, 4594)\n}\n\n/* ── ch27's node table, encoded as CODE so the co-evolution rule is checked at registration\n      rather than reviewed by eye. Straight from ch27 \"The co-evolution rule\".              */\ntype ProcessId = 'hydraulic' | 'thermal' | 'aeolian' | 'glacial' | 'weathering'\n               | 'orographic' | 'snow'\nconst CO_EVOLUTION: Record<ProcessId, AuxName[]> = {\n  hydraulic:  ['sedimentDepth', 'soilDepth', 'wetness', 'flowVelocity', 'waterDepth'],\n  thermal:    ['soilDepth'],\n  aeolian:    ['sandDepth', 'moisture'],\n  glacial:    ['soilDepth', 'wetness', 'flowVelocity'],\n  weathering: ['soilDepth'],\n  orographic: ['moisture'],\n  snow:       ['snowDepth', 'wetness', 'soilDepth'],\n}\n\n/* ── ParamDesc: today's 10 P.* constructors (index.html:4015-4031) produce 7 type strings\n      across 235 declarations. Everything below is ADDITIVE — nothing reads the new fields in\n      Phase A, so nothing can move.                                                         */\ninterface ParamDesc {\n  key: string; label: string\n  type: 'slider' | 'number' | 'select' | 'seg' | 'tabs' | 'text' | 'curve' | 'seed'\n      | 'layerStack' | 'strokes'\n  //  'layerStack' and 'strokes' close the two holes where `def.params` is NOT a complete\n  //  description of node state: colormixer's params.layers and drawmask's params.strokes are\n  //  both created in makeNode (index.html:4722-4727) and both had to be hardcoded in the\n  //  oracle (_verify_digest.js:158-164). Without these two types the plugin loader ships with\n  //  escape hatches on day one.\n  min?: number; max?: number; def: unknown; step?: number\n  scale?: 'log'; floor?: number; zero?: boolean\n  opts?: Array<[string, string]> | null   // null = resolve from a live registry (satmap\n                                          // gradient, index.html:4600 → SATMAPS at :985)\n  fmt?: (v: number) => string             // 63 inline closures; 13 close over terrainDef.\n                                          // Kept as CODE — this is why the manifest is a data\n                                          // half plus a code half, not JSON.\n  when?: { key: string; values: string[] }   // WHEN, index.html:4034, 20 uses / 8 nodes\n  group?: string                             // GROUP, index.html:4035, 40 uses / 5 nodes\n  // ── new, declarative only ──\n  unit?: 'm' | 'm²' | 'deg' | 'tan' | 'm/yr' | '1/yr' | 'cells⚠' | 'ratio' | 'none'\n  //  ch14:83-87. `cells⚠` is a DECLARED resolution-dependence and forces RESOLUTION_BOUND.\n  //  Six params are raw cell counts multiplied by resScale() at dispatch (blur.radius:4233,\n  //  d_deposits.radius:4554, d_peaks.radius:4561, sculpt.radius, hydraulic.radius:4359 which\n  //  is literally labelled \" px\"); ten more are iteration counts, which ch14:108-111 calls a\n  //  smell. Declaring them is free; consuming the declaration is not (see risks).\n  invalidates?: 'VALUE' | 'TOPOLOGY' | 'COMPOSE_ONLY'\n  //  ch14:173-177. COMPOSE_ONLY is not in ch14 — it is measured HERE: canyonCacheKey\n  //  (index.html:2130-2136) deliberately excludes `depth` because depth is an affine gain\n  //  over the cached solve and \"never re-runs the process\" (:2252-2258). That is the only\n  //  content-addressed cache in the app and it is the template to generalise.\n  quantise?: number       // ch14:161 — float params quantised before hashing, or a slider\n                          // drag generates one never-reused cache entry per pixel. Today\n                          // canyonCacheKey joins RAW floats.\n  promotableToField?: boolean   // ch14:94-97 fieldRef — declare it now, wire it in Phase C.\n}\n\n/* ── The plugin. `cat/name/ins/desc/params` are the existing mandatory 5; the rest is either\n      an existing optional key promoted to first class, or a ch14/ch27 flag that is dead data\n      in Phase A.                                                                            */\ninterface PluginDesc {\n  // ---- identity -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n  typeId: string           // the TYPES key. NOT versioned yet: ch14:23 wants \"erosion.pipe/2\"\n                           // but the key is the digest baseline's key (60 keys in\n                           // _digest_baseline.json) — versioning it is a Phase C rename.\n  cat: 'gen'|'comb'|'filt'|'ero'|'mask'|'data'|'effect'|'out'   // CAT, index.html:4036-4038\n  name: string\n  desc: string             // 6,732 chars across 60, zero markup — safe as data\n  note?: string            // 22 nodes; HTML — stays a string\n  info?: (nd: NodeInstance) => string\n  //  EXACTLY ONE node uses this (transform, index.html:4270-4273) and it reads nd._xfMode,\n  //  which transform's own eval wrote. It is the proof TYPES is code, not JSON. Kept, but\n  //  narrowed: `info` may read only declared derived state, never arbitrary node internals.\n\n  // ---- ports ------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n  ins: string[]            // ★ ORDER IS DIGEST-LOAD-BEARING. The oracle wires a DISTINCT\n                           // perlin seed per slot (_verify_digest.js:198-254) precisely so a\n                           // slot swap moves the digest. Never reorder. Never append aux\n                           // maps here — see `reads` below.\n  variadic?: { min: number; max: number; label: string }\n  //  colormixer only (2-15). Its port count is INSTANCE state (nd._inputs overrides def.ins\n  //  via nodeInputs, index.html:4050), so `ins` is genuinely not a type-level property for\n  //  it. Declaring variadic is what stops that being a special case in the loader.\n  referenceOnly?: number[]   // drawmask:[0] (index.html:4096) — a slot whose value is shown,\n                             // not consumed. Honoured by exactChain:4054 and evalExact:4062,\n                             // AND by the oracle (_verify_digest.js:186), but NOT by either\n                             // in-app evaluator. The contract makes the RUNTIME authoritative\n                             // and drawmask's own `nd._reference=ins[0]` becomes redundant.\n  writes?: AuxPort[]         // ch27 First-Class Citizen Rule\n  reads?: AuxName[]          // aux maps consumed from upstream, on a SIDE CHANNEL\n                             // (ctx.inputAux), never as new `ins` slots.\n  scalarsOut?: ScalarPort[]\n  process?: ProcessId        // ★ the co-evolution trigger. Declaring it ARMS the registration\n                             // assertion below.\n\n  // ---- semantics ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n  fieldSemantics?: 'preserve-primary' | 'temperature-pair'\n  //  5 nodes today (warp:4205, blur:4231, transform:4245 / blend:4173, maxmin:4179). Read by\n  //  propagateFieldMetadata (index.html:3225-3235). The default is DROP, deliberately\n  //  (comment at :3222-3224) — a migration that defaults to preserve changes 55 node types.\n  effect?: 'water'|'snow'|'satmap'|'colorerosion'|'weathering'|'satmapblend'|'colormixer'\n  //  Declared on 7 nodes today and READ NOWHERE (zero occurrences of `.effect` in 8,112\n  //  lines). The real dispatch is two hardcoded `nd.type===` chains: resolveColor:6108-6125\n  //  and collectScene:4740/4747/4759. Promoting this key to the actual discriminator is\n  //  behaviour-identical and is the cheapest commit that makes colour a declared capability.\n  heightPassthrough?: boolean   // ── the SPLIT. `passthrough:true` has exactly one read site\n  colorPassthrough?: boolean    //    (markParamDirty, index.html:4781, 7 node types) while\n  //  the evaluators carry a SECOND, inconsistent rule — `nd.type!==\"water\"&&nd.type!==\"snow\"`\n  //  (index.html:4848 and :4883, 2 node types). They are two classifications of one concept\n  //  and they disagree by construction. The contract carries both facets separately: \"my\n  //  params do not change height\" vs \"my params do not change colour\".\n  exactUnderTransform?: boolean\n  //  Replaces EXACT_TYPES, a hand-maintained Set of 18 type-id strings living OUTSIDE the\n  //  registry (index.html:4048-4049). If this does not move into the manifest in Phase A,\n  //  Phase B leaves a hardcoded type-name list inside core/graph.ts, which defeats\n  //  plugin-per-node outright.\n\n  // ---- ch14 flags (dead data in Phase A) ---------------------------------------------------\n  locality: 'LOCAL' | { NEIGHBOURHOOD: number } | 'GLOBAL'   // ch14:48-53, the tiling contract\n  resolution: 'RESOLUTION_INVARIANT' | 'RESOLUTION_BOUND'    // ch14:55-59, the preview contract\n  scalingPolicy?: (p: Params, ctx: EvalContext) => Params\n  //  ch14:197-199 — RESOLUTION_BOUND nodes MUST declare how they adjust at preview. Today\n  //  this is `const k=resScale();` inside 6 evals and BUILD_QUALITY reads inside 3 more\n  //  (thermal:4286/4295, streampower:4331/4332, hydraulic:4362/4372). Declaring it is free;\n  //  MOVING the resScale() call out of eval is not — the rounding\n  //  `Math.max(1,Math.round(p.radius*resScale()))` is inside the digest.\n  device: 'CPU_ONLY' | 'GPU_NATIVE' | 'EITHER'\n  gpuLattice?: 'square'\n  //  gpuReady() is `USE_GPU && terrainDef.lattice!==\"hex\" && GPU.init()` (index.html:1502),\n  //  called from 6 node evals directly (perlin:4072, simplex:4076, ridged:4080, warp:4208,\n  //  thermal:4298, hydraulic:4363) plus erosion2 via erosion2Field:3938. The GPU path forces\n  //  square textures; the hex disable is a correctness gate, not an optimisation. Note the\n  //  digest pins USE_GPU=false (_verify_digest.js:110), so the CPU branch alone is gated —\n  //  _verify_gpu.js is the companion gate and must run alongside.\n  determinism: 'DETERMINISTIC' | 'STOCHASTIC'   // ch14:27\n\n  // ---- code --------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n  params: ParamDesc[]\n  evaluate(ctx: EvalContext): Outputs\n  color?(ctx: ColorContext): Float32Array | null\n  //  the second output kind. 5 of 60 nodes (satmap, colorerosion, weathering, satmapblend,\n  //  colormixer) do essentially nothing in eval — satmapblend's ENTIRE eval is\n  //  `nd._mask=ins[2]||null; return ins[0]||newField();` (index.html:4642) — and their real\n  //  work runs in resolveColor's separate walk. Without this member those 5 cannot be\n  //  plugins at all. Gated: _verify_digest.js:378-385 folds resolveColor's RGB into their\n  //  digests, so the walk order and memo are pinned.\n}\n\n/* ── Registration. definePlugin is a VALIDATOR + a PROJECTOR, and both halves matter.       */\ndeclare function definePlugin(d: PluginDesc): PluginDesc & LegacyTypeEntry\n\n/* Validation, at registration (boot), throwing — see `coEvolution` for the full argument:\n   1. if `process` is set and `writes` contains 'height', then CO_EVOLUTION[process] ⊆\n      writes.map(w => w.name), or THROW. A node that alters terrain without declaring the\n      auxiliary maps its physical process touches cannot be registered.\n   2. any AuxPort with kind:'derived' may not be listed in `reads` of a downstream node —\n      derived maps are recomputed, never carried (ch27 lifecycle).\n   3. any param with unit 'cells⚠' forces resolution === 'RESOLUTION_BOUND'.\n   4. `writes` containing a conserved quantity requires evaluate() to return `budget`.\n   5. exactUnderTransform must agree with the frozen EXACT_TYPES set during Phase A (a\n      transitional assertion that deletes itself when EXACT_TYPES does).\n\n   PROJECTION — the legacy shape the oracle and 23 in-app `TYPES[` read sites still see:      */\ntype LegacyTypeEntry = {\n  cat: string; name: string; ins: string[]; desc: string; params: ParamDesc[]\n  eval: (p: Params, ins: (Float32Array | null)[], nd: NodeInstance) => Float32Array\n  note?: string; passthrough?: boolean; effect?: string\n  fieldSemantics?: string; referenceOnly?: number[]; info?: (nd: NodeInstance) => string\n}\n/* The generated `eval` does exactly six things, in this order, and nothing else:\n     1. ctx = currentEvalContext(nd, ins)          — reads the INSTALLED context (never\n                                                     re-derives res/xf, so re-entrancy holds)\n     2. out = d.evaluate(ctx)\n     3. for each port in d.writes with a legacySlot: assign out.aux[port.name] to that dotted\n        path on `nd` — SAME array instance, no copy, no wrapper\n     4. for each ScalarPort with a legacySlot: assign out.scalars[name]\n     5. nd._inputError = out.error ?? null ; nd._budget = out.budget ?? null\n        (and, transitionally, hydroMassDiag = out.budget?.[0] ?? hydroMassDiag)\n     6. return out.height ?? ctx.newField()\n   Step 3's \"same array instance\" is not a style note: FIELD_META is a WeakMap keyed on the\n   Float32Array OBJECT (index.html:3202). One copy anywhere drops every temperature tag and\n   tempmask / d_heat / snow / water fall into their \"Connect a Temperature field\" branches. */\nEVAL CONTEXT:\nWHAT IT CARRIES — exactly the eight hidden inputs I measured, plus four capabilities. Nothing else, because the survey's transitive reachability from the TYPES literal (index.html:4068-4651) bottoms out at four MUTABLE globals — `RES` (:972), `terrainDef` (:6133), `SCALE_RES` (:1219), `XF` (:1109) — and four module consts that behave as hidden inputs because they are never passed: `HEX_SQUARE_WORLD` (:1059), `REF_RES`=192 (:1220), `HEX_ROW` (:6143), `BUILD_QUALITY` (:1222). Two of the four mutables are WRITTEN during evaluation: `RES` by atFeatureScale (:2611, `RES=w;try{out=fn(small);}finally{RES=prev;} // the kernels read the global RES`) and `XF` by transform's own eval (:4263-4264). Those two writes are the entire reason a frozen `ctx` breaks behaviour.\n\n```ts\ninterface EvalContext {\n  readonly nodeId: NodeId; readonly typeId: string\n\n  // ── grid (the four mutables + four consts, unified into one readable surface) ──\n  readonly res: number        // === RES (:972)\n  readonly rows: number       // === fieldH() (:1066) = hex ? round(res*2/√3) : res\n  readonly len: number        // res*rows — the ONLY legal field length (oracle asserts it)\n  readonly lattice: Lattice   // terrainDef.lattice (:6134)\n  readonly cellSizeM: number  // terrainDef.scale/res (:6135)\n  readonly resScale: number   // SCALE_RES ? res/REF_RES : 1 (:1221), 18 call sites\n  readonly quality: Quality   // BUILD_QUALITY (:1222), 14 reads\n  readonly xf: Affine | null  // XF (:1109), read per-sample by xfU/xfV (:1110-1111)\n  readonly world: Readonly<TerrainDef>   // a LIVE VIEW of terrainDef, never a copy\n\n  // ── lattice abstraction (ch26) ──\n  readonly L: LatticeOps\n  newField(v?: number): Float32Array     // === index.html:1068, length === this.len\n\n  // ── the two mid-eval rebindings, given names ──\n  withResolution<T>(w: number, fn: (c: EvalContext) => T): T   // atFeatureScale (:2606-2616)\n  withTransform<T>(xf: Affine, fn: (c: EvalContext) => T): T   // transform.eval (:4263-4264)\n\n  // ── graph capabilities ──\n  input(slot: number): Float32Array | null\n  inputAux(slot: number, port: AuxName): Float32Array | null\n  inputMeta(slot: number): FieldMeta | null          // FIELD_META (:3202)\n  canEvaluateExact(slot: number): boolean            // exactChain (:4051-4056)\n  evaluateUpstream(slot: number, o?: {exact?: boolean}): Float32Array | null  // evalExact (:4059)\n\n  // ── determinism, device, scheduling ──\n  readonly rootSeed: number\n  rng(salt?: string): () => number                   // hash(rootSeed, nodeId, seedOffset)\n  readonly device: 'cpu' | 'gpu'\n  gpu(): GpuBackend | null                           // null on hex / USE_GPU=false\n  ledger: MassLedger\n  progress(frac: number, label?: string): void\n  readonly cancelled: boolean                        // the buildRun generation counter (:4870)\n}\n\ninterface LatticeOps {                    // pure; imports nothing that reads document state\n  readonly rowPitch: number               // isHex() ? HEX_ROW : 1 — the idiom appears 10×\n  parityShift(y: number): number          // hex ? 0.5*(y&1) : 0 — the idiom appears 8×\n  neighbours(y: number): ReadonlyArray<[number, number]>   // hexNb(y) (:6174), the D6 one-ring\n  readonly gradientBasis: { ex: number[]; ey: number[] }   // HEX_EX/HEX_EY (:6178), Σeₖeₖᵀ=3I\n  rowsFor(w: number): number              // latticeRows(w) (:1064)\n  sampleBilinear(f: Float32Array, x: number, y: number): number   // (:1069-1090)\n  domainUV(x: number, y: number): [number, number]\n}\n```\n\nHOW A PLUGIN GETS THE LATTICE. Through `ctx.L` only. `LatticeOps` is constructed from `(ctx.lattice, ctx.res)` and closes over nothing mutable. That resolves the split the survey identified: the correct cut is THREE files, not two — `lattice-constants` (HEX_ROW/HEX_NB_EVEN/HEX_NB_ODD/HEX_EX/HEX_EY: pure, zero imports), `document-state` (terrainDef/RES/SCALE_RES/BUILD_QUALITY/XF), and the derived predicates (isHex/cellSizeM/latticeRows/fieldW/fieldH) which read state and therefore belong with `field`, not with the constants. Import direction is fixed once, up front: constants ← nothing, state ← nothing, derived ← both, kernels ← derived. Otherwise `field.ts` and `lattice.ts` become mutually importing the first time someone \"tidies\" `latticeRows` into the file where `terrainDef` lives. `ctx.L` must also be importable by the GPU layer, which is why it cannot depend on anything CPU-field-shaped.\n\nHOW A PLUGIN GETS AUX MAPS. `ctx.inputAux(slot, name)` — a SIDE CHANNEL, deliberately not new `ins` slots. This is forced by the gate: the oracle wires a distinct perlin seed into every declared slot (`SEEDS = {A:101,B:202,C:303,M:404,D:505}`, `WIRING` table at _verify_digest.js:225-254) precisely so a slot swap moves the digest. Appending aux ports to `ins` would change `nodeInputs(nd).length` and move the digest of every node it touched, for zero behavioural reason. So aux maps travel alongside the edge — `inputAux(slot,name)` resolves the upstream node's declared `writes` bundle for that port — and `ins` order stays frozen forever. Producing is symmetric: the plugin returns `Outputs.aux`, and the runtime scatters it to `legacySlot` for the oracle and stores it on the port for downstream `inputAux`.\n\nHOW A PLUGIN GETS RNG. `ctx.rng(salt)` derived from `hash(rootSeed, nodeId, seedOffset)` per ch14:88-90, so re-rolling one node never disturbs another. Honest Phase A status: this is ADDITIVE AND UNUSED. All 11 `seed` params today pass their raw integer straight into the kernels (`fbmField(gnoise,{seed:p.seed,…})`, index.html:4072), and hydraulicErode seeds its own LCG from it (`rng=Math.imul(seed|0,2654435761)>>>0`, :3825-3826). Every one of those is inside a digest. `ctx.rng` exists in Phase A so Phase B plugins have a legal RNG; converting an existing node to it is a deliberate, digest-moving act.\n\nWHY IT IS INSTALLED, NOT THREADED. In Phase A the evaluator does `withEvalContext(ctx, () => def.eval(...))`, setting a module-scoped current context that `newField()` / `fieldW()` / `fieldH()` / `cellSizeM()` / `resScale()` read. The 104 nullary `newField()` call sites are not touched — and must not be, because `newField()` is the single most-called symbol in the app, its return LENGTH is a function of two mutable globals, and the digest is length-sensitive: one missed site returns a short array and drifts its node silently. `withResolution` / `withTransform` are then push/pop on that context stack, which reproduces atFeatureScale's and transform's dynamic scoping EXACTLY rather than approximating it. The plugin additionally receives the same object explicitly as `evaluate(ctx)`. That dual access is temporary and it is safe only under one invariant: there is exactly ONE source of truth. The proof that this matters is already in the tree — `simulateTerrainWind(inp,p,context)` takes `context.terrainDef` at index.html:3444-3445 and then reads the GLOBAL `terrainDef.lattice` two lines later at :3465, and `latticeRows(n)` at :3445 takes `n` as a parameter while still reading the global lattice internally. That leak is invisible to the digest, because the oracle assigns into the LIVE terrainDef (`Object.assign(terrainDef, {...})`, _verify_digest.js:114-115) and so never creates a context that disagrees with the global. Rule for the contract: any ctx field that a PRIMITIVE also reads globally must be removed from the global read path in the same commit it enters the context, or the two will disagree and nothing will notice.\nCO-EVOLUTION: Five enforcement mechanisms, in increasing order of how hard they are to evade. Documentation is not one of them.\n\n1. STRUCTURAL — \"in the same pass, from the same intermediate state\" is made unrepresentable otherwise. `evaluate(ctx)` returns ONE `Outputs` bundle and there is no other emission path: `EvalContext` exposes no aux writer, no store handle, no `emit()`. A plugin physically cannot compute soilDepth in a later pass, because after `evaluate` returns it never sees the aux store again. This is the direct structural answer to the failure already in the tree — `hydroMassDiag` (index.html:1443) is a module-level, last-writer-wins global holding hydraulic erosion's whole mass budget, written by the GPU pipes at :1495 and the droplets at :3906 with no node identity, so two erosion nodes in one graph destroy the first one's ledger. It exists ONLY because the signature returns one Float32Array and has nowhere else to put what the sim computed. That is ch27's co-evolution rule failing in the concrete, and it is the strongest single argument for `(params, inputs, ctx) -> {height, ...auxMaps}` over \"field-returning function plus node mutation\".\n\n2. REGISTRATION-TIME — ch27's node table is encoded as `CO_EVOLUTION: Record<ProcessId, AuxName[]>` and `definePlugin` THROWS at boot when `process` is declared, `writes` contains 'height', and the required co-update set is not a subset of `writes`. ch27 says \"A node that changed height without touching the maps its process implies fails review by inspection — the output-port list is the tell.\" This turns \"fails review by inspection\" into \"fails registration\". A8 makes it non-vacuous immediately by declaring `process` on the seven nodes ch27's table names (thermal, hydraulic, streampower, erosion2, hydrofix, snow, weathering), so the assertion has teeth from the commit it lands in rather than waiting for a future plugin to opt in.\n\n3. LIFECYCLE-TIME — state vs derived is a TYPE, and the runtime treats them oppositely. A `kind:'state'` port flows on the edge side channel and its absence upstream is an error, never a silent zero default; it may never be recomputed from final geometry. A `kind:'derived'` port may NOT appear in any downstream node's `reads` — it is recomputed from the current cone, keyed on the height key, and is stale by definition the moment its input cone changes (ch14:140-154's Merkle key makes staleness detectable by construction, which is precisely what ch27 tells you to use). The app is already partly compliant and the contract should not disturb that: d_deposits, d_flow, d_wear and d_texture are genuine derived maps recomputed from final height, and must not be mistaken for state maps.\n\n4. BUDGET-TIME — every plugin whose `writes` includes a conserved quantity must return `budget: MassBudget[]`, and the runtime asserts closure in dev/test. `MassBudget`'s shape is literally today's droplet ledger (`{engine, settle, sumIn, sumOut, settled, exported, exportedDerived, lost, brushClipGain}`, index.html:3906) generalised, with ch27's crucial refinement: sum the STACK, not the layers separately, because the layers legitimately exchange mass. The gate already exists — `_verify_erosion_mass.js` runs G1..G5c against `hydroMassDiag`, was written RED-FIRST against the pre-fix build, and its header records the actual red-run numbers (GPU deficit 0.9193 → ~0.35; CPU 0.4291 with 41.2% of it legitimate boundary export; the +1.25-unit `brushClipGain` mass SOURCE that a 5% tolerance was quietly absorbing). Generalising that verifier to ch27's `assert_layer_budget` semantics is a small, natural step, not a new discipline.\n\n5. GATE-TIME — `_verify_coevolution.js` prints the debt ledger at every commit: per process, how many required co-update maps are declared and how many are implemented. Compliance becomes a measured number rather than a claim. Paired with A2's component-aware digest, each newly-implemented aux port surfaces as an explicit \"component added\" review event on exactly one node.\n\nWHAT THIS COSTS PHASE A, HONESTLY.\n\nIt costs three commits (A8, A10, and the `writes`/`reads`/`process` half of A7), one new manifest concept, one registration validator, one new gate file, one oracle upgrade — and ZERO lines of numeric code. Nothing about the 60 nodes' arithmetic changes, so the digest cannot move, and every one of those steps is `digestSafe: true`.\n\nWhat it buys is that the 13 node-mutating evals get migrated EXACTLY ONCE (A9) rather than twice. Bolt ch27 on after Phase B and you touch all 60 plugins a second time; declare the port model now and the aux migration is a per-node fill-in-the-blank against a contract that already exists.\n\nWHAT IT DOES NOT BUY, ALSO HONESTLY. No auxiliary map is actually COMPUTED in Phase A. soilDepth, sedimentDepth, sandDepth, wetness, moisture, flowVelocity and waterDepth are absent as ports today and stay absent — they land in A8 as declared debt with `implemented:false`. Implementing each one is Phase B work that moves that node's aux components (reviewable via A2, never silent). And there is one design consequence forced by the gate that must be stated up front: aux maps CANNOT ride on `ins`. The oracle wires a distinct perlin seed into every declared slot precisely so a slot swap moves the digest (`SEEDS`/`WIRING`, _verify_digest.js:198-254), so appending aux ports to `ins` would change `nodeInputs(nd).length` and move digests for zero behavioural reason. ch27's \"persistent field travelling through the DAG on a typed port\" is therefore delivered as a SIDE CHANNEL alongside the edge (`ctx.inputAux(slot, name)`), with `ins` order frozen permanently. That is not a compromise of ch27 — the port is still typed, persistent and edge-carried — but it is a real constraint the hard gate imposes on the shape of the solution, and pretending otherwise would produce a design that goes red on its first commit.\nDIGEST SAFETY: MECHANICALLY, the digest cannot move because the oracle's entire contact surface with the app is ENUMERABLE, and every Phase A step preserves all of it. I read `_verify_digest.js` end to end; the surface is exactly this:\n\nWRITES into the page (installHarness, :104-117, :147-151): `buildRun++`, `cancelAnimationFrame(evalFrame)`, `RES`, `TARGET_RES`, `USE_GPU=false`, `SCALE_RES=true`, `BUILD_QUALITY='interactive'`, `XF=null`, `Object.assign(terrainDef,{scale:5000,height:2600,baseElevation:0,latitude:46,north:0,seaTemp:6,lapseRate:6.5,solarElevation:45})`, `H_SCALE`, `nodes.length=0`, `edges.length=0`, `uid=1`, `selected=null`, `selectedEdge=null`, `CANYON_EVOLUTION_CACHE.clear()`.\n\nREADS: `TYPES` (its key list, and per entry `.params[].key` / `.params[].def`, `.eval`, `.referenceOnly`), `cloneParams`, `nodeById`, `inputEdge`, `nodeInputs`, `propagateFieldMetadata`, `resolveColor`, `priorityFloodFill`, `d8Accumulation`.\n\nNODE SHAPE (mk, :152-168): id, type, x, y, w, params, _field, _thumb, _dirty, _dem, _snowLayer, _temperatureC, _solarShadow, _solarExposure, _wind, and `_inputs` for colormixer.\n\nOUTPUT SHAPE ASSERTIONS: `ArrayBuffer.isView(primary)` (:350) and `primary.length === RES*RES` (:354). Failing either is not a soft warning — the node is SKIPPED, which the gate then reports as `missing`, which is a FAIL.\n\nAUXILIARY: dotted-path reads off the node object via `reach()` (:276) for the 7 AUX entries (:259-267) and the 7 snow SCALARS (:271-275); `resolveColor(nd.id, RES, {}, new Set())` (:380) for the 5 COLOUR types; `priorityFloodFill`/`d8Accumulation` for water (:394-396); and a second non-default evaluation for the 8 EXERCISE types (:286-298).\n\nSo digest immobility is a five-clause invariant, and every cut-list step is a move that provably preserves all five:\n\nI1 — `TYPES` stays a live object whose 60 entries expose `.eval(params, ins, nd) -> Float32Array` of length `fieldLen()`. This is why `definePlugin` PROJECTS the legacy shape instead of replacing it, and why `evaluate(ctx) -> Outputs` arrives behind a GENERATED `eval` shim (A9) rather than as a signature change. A naive \"change eval to return {primary, aux}\" would make `primary` a plain object at :350, skip all 14 affected types, and turn 60/60 into 46/60 — a FAIL that reads as an absence rather than a drift.\n\nI2 — the legacy `_`-slots are still populated by the time `run()` reads them. Guaranteed by `AuxPort.legacySlot`, which the shim scatters to. The oracle compares digest VALUES, not access paths, so it keeps finding `nd._temperatureC`, `nd._wind.u`, `nd._snowLayer.depthM`, `nd._driver` exactly where it looks, and no oracle edit is needed.\n\nI3 — no float expression is re-associated, re-ordered or hoisted. Enforced by rule, not by hope: A3 forbids hoisting explicitly, because `(x+hxs*(y&1))/n` and `x/n+hxs*(y&1)/n` do not round identically, and that idiom sits in eight per-pixel loops the digest hashes.\n\nI4 — the ambient values at eval time are identical, INCLUDING the two mid-eval rebindings. `withResolution` / `withTransform` (A5) are push/pop on the installed context, which reproduces `RES=w;try{…}finally{RES=prev}` (:2611) and `XF=xfMul(…);try{…}finally{XF=prev}` (:4263-4264) exactly. A frozen ctx would silently change thermal, streampower, hydraulic, erosion2 and transform — and erosion2 genuinely enters the path in the gate (erosionScale default .38 → featureScale 3.28 → RES rebound to 78 at the digest's 256).\n\nI5 — the page-side names above stay resolvable. That is what A1's bridge repair is for, and it is why A1 is first.\n\nWHERE 60/60 IS NOT EVIDENCE — the gate's blind spots, measured:\n- GPU: `USE_GPU=false` (:110). Six node evals branch on `gpuReady()` (index.html:1502) and NONE of those branches is hashed. `_verify_gpu.js` is the companion gate.\n- LATTICE: the gate is structurally square-only. `primary.length !== RES*RES` skips a node (:354), and on hex `fieldH()` = round(256·2/√3) = 296 ≠ 256, so a hex sweep would skip all sixty and report `missing`, not `drift`. 60/60 says nothing whatsoever about hex.\n- PREVIEW PYRAMID: at RES=256, `Math.min(full,512)` collapses to preview===full, so the 512-cap branch in evaluateSunShadowMap/evaluateTemperatureMap/evaluateTerrainWind/evaluateSnowLayer never executes, and neither do its 25 square-only `resampleField` sites.\n- INVALIDATION: the graph is torn down per node type with `_dirty:true` everywhere, so markDirtyFrom / markParamDirty / applyWorkingResolution / the terrainDef slider handlers are exercised zero times. The measured under-invalidation (canyon reads terrainDef.scale/height in its cache key at :2130-2136 but the canyon NODE is never re-dirtied on a terrainDef edit) is invisible to it.\n- CACHE-HIT COST: four caches keyed by Float32Array REFERENCE identity (`_colorErosionRoute` :5978, `_weatherSignal` :6031, `_biomeMaskCache` :6094, and the viewport's `curField`/`curSnowLayerRef` :6819) all miss forever if a purified evaluator returns fresh arrays for unchanged inputs — a silent 5-50× regression the gate passes cleanly, because it builds one node type at a time from a torn-down graph.\n- CYCLES: every digest graph is acyclic. `evalGraph`'s `ev()` (:4840, feeds a null input and caches with `_dirty=false`) and `graphEvalOrder()` (:4854, drops the back-edge in `visiting`) resolve cycles DIFFERENTLY and neither is pinned.\n- CONTEXT DISAGREEMENT: the oracle assigns into the LIVE terrainDef (`Object.assign`, :114), so it never constructs a context whose terrainDef differs from the global — which is exactly why the simulateTerrainWind leak (:3444 vs :3465) is invisible today.\n\nTHE ONE PLACE PHASE A IS HONESTLY NOT \"UNCHANGED\": implementing a new aux map changes that node's digest STRING, because the string is a composite and a new component is appended. That is not drift of the primary, and conflating the two would force a choice between shipping ch27 and keeping the gate green. A2 removes the conflict: the comparator becomes component-aware, so `primary unchanged + N components added` is a distinguishable, reviewable verdict with an explicit re-baseline, and the gate never goes red. A2 itself is byte-neutral — same strings in, same verdicts out — so it lands green.\nCUT LIST:\n  1. This is the ONE commit where the oracle's page-side accessors are allowed to change — _verify_digest.js:60-71 says so in writing, and its own header names 'the oracle went blind' as the worst available failure mode. Sequence it first or every later measurement is taken through a broken instrument.\n  3. A2 · Make the digest oracle component-aware (so ch27 aux ports are reviewable, not red) [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: A node's digest is a composite string — `parts.join('|')` at _verify_digest.js:411, of the form `<primary>|name=<digest>|…|color=<digest>|ex=<digest>`. Teach the gate to PARSE it into a component map and report per-component verdicts: `primary drifted` = FAIL; `component removed` = FAIL; `component ADDED` = REVIEW + explicit re-baseline. Add `--component=primary` for a strict primary-only comparison. Also derive the `AUX`/`SCALARS` tables (currently hardcoded at :259-275) from `TYPES[t].writes`/`scalarsOut` once A7 lands, so new ports auto-extend coverage instead of being silently uncovered.\n     GATE: Byte-identical verdict on today's baseline: `git diff --exit-code tests/legacy/_digest_baseline.json` must be empty and the gate must still print PASS 60. Plus a mutation drill — perturb one kernel constant in a scratch copy and assert EXACTLY ONE component of ONE node moves, so the new comparator is proven sensitive rather than merely quiet. Without this step, the first implemented aux map (Phase B) is indistinguishable from drift, and the pressure will be to skip the port.\n  4. A3 · Free no-op normalisations, proven byte-identical [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: `gAt` (index.html:2765) clamps rows with `fieldH()` but strides columns with a bare `RES`: `const gAt=(f,x,y)=>f[clamp(y,0,fieldH()-1)*RES+clamp(x,0,RES-1)];` — correct only because `fieldW()` is defined as `()=>RES` (:1065). Normalise it to `fieldW()`, 15 call sites of `gAt(` unaffected. Then route the 19 bare `terrainDef.lattice===\"hex\"` reads through `isHex()` where isHex is in scope. HOISTING AND RE-ASSOCIATION ARE FORBIDDEN in this step: `(x+hxs*(y&1))/n` is not the same rounding as `x/n+hxs*(y&1)/n`, and the odd-row half-cell idiom sits in 8 per-pixel loops the digest hashes (fbmField:1158, worleyField:1173, gradientField:1190, radialField:1196, drawMaskField:1548, mountainField:1980, warpField:2650, buildIndex:6768).\n     GATE: Digest 60/60 — and this one is genuinely free, because on square `fieldW()===RES` is an identity substitution. Companion gates for the lattice half, all newly reachable after A1: `_verify_hex_parity.js`, `_verify_hex_sampling.js`, `_verify_flip.js`, `_verify_hex_flow.js`. Do it BEFORE any structural move: the day `fieldW()` stops aliasing RES (a tiled field, a rect authoring domain, a per-node working grid) `gAt` strides wrong and every consumer drifts at once.\n  5. A4 · Fix the declaration-order inversion, still inside index.html [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: Every field primitive forward-references a lattice block declared 5,000+ lines BELOW it: `latticeRows` (:1064) reads `terrainDef` (:6133); `sampleBilinear` (:1071,:1081) reads terrainDef and `HEX_ROW` (:6143); `hexNb` (:6174) is first CALLED at :2745. It works today only because nothing executes until `boot()` at :8109. Move the block at :6133-6179 — terrainDef, cellSizeM, isHex, HEX_ROW, HEX_NB_EVEN/ODD, hexNb, HEX_EX/HEX_EY, H_SCALE — up to just above :1059, and split it into three ordered regions (lattice-constants → document-state → derived predicates). I verified by reading :6129-6186 that this range has ZERO backward references beyond `RES` (:972), so the move is mechanical. Keep HEX_EY immediately after HEX_ROW (HEX_EY reads it at module-init). Leave `uTime`/`TEMP_UNIT`/`REDUCED`/`scene`/`SHADE` (:6179-6185) where they are — they are renderer/UI state, not core.\n     GATE: Digest 60/60, and specifically its page-error channel: `_verify_digest.js:468` collects `pageerror`, and a TDZ violation surfaces there as a ReferenceError, not as a wrong number — so the mechanical proof of 'no init-order break' is `page errors observed (0)` in the gate's stderr. Plus the full hex + wireframe + render set. Do this before the ESM split: splitting by section banner without moving these produces ReferenceErrors, not wrong numbers, and a naive line-order split makes field.ts and lattice.ts mutually importing.\n  6. A5 · Introduce EvalContext as an INSTALLED object and name the two rebindings [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: Create the ctx {res, rows, len, lattice, cellSizeM, resScale, quality, xf, world} where every field is a GETTER over the existing global — one source of truth, so ctx and globals cannot disagree (this is the fix for the simulateTerrainWind leak at :3444-3465, and it is a fix by construction, not by discipline). Add `withEvalContext(ctx, fn)`, `withResolution(w, fn)`, `withTransform(xf, fn)`. Rewrite atFeatureScale (:2606-2616) to `withResolution(w, () => fn(small))` and transform.eval (:4258-4269) to `withTransform(xfMul(xfFromParams(p),prev), () => evalExact(src.from,new Set()))`. Do NOT change `newField()`'s nullary signature — 104 call sites, length-sensitive digest.\n     GATE: Digest 60/60, and here the gate is genuinely sensitive rather than lucky: erosion2's default `erosionScale` is .38 (index.html:4383), so featureScale = lerp(1,7,.38) = 3.28 (:3924), tier = max(1,256/384) = 1 at the digest's RES, and 3.28 > 1.02 — so `atFeatureScale` DOES rebind RES to 78 inside the digest run. Transform is doubly pinned: the default takes the exact path and the oracle's EXERCISE entry forces `mode:'raster'` (_verify_digest.js:291-292) so both branches are hashed. Companion gates: `_verify_featurescale.js`, `_verify_exact_transform.js`, `_verify_erosion_gridscale.js`. Note thermal (:4285) and hydraulic (:4361) default feat=1 and do NOT exercise the rebinding — say so rather than claiming full coverage.\n  7. A6 · definePlugin() lands as a pure projection — 60 entries, zero semantics [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: Add `definePlugin` and convert `const TYPES={…}` (index.html:4068-4651) into a registry of 60 `definePlugin({…})` calls whose fields are passed straight through, `eval` still the identical closure. Nothing new is READ yet. The full key union across all 60 is exactly 12 keys — cat, name, ins, desc, params, eval (mandatory on 60/60) + note×22, passthrough×7, effect×7, fieldSemantics×5, referenceOnly×1, info×1 — so the projection is total, not approximate.\n     GATE: Digest 60/60 + a new `_verify_manifest.js` asserting: TYPES has exactly 60 keys; each has the 6 mandatory keys; the eval arity histogram is unchanged (36×`(p,ins)`, 11×`(p,ins,nd)`, 10×`(p)`, 3×`(p,ins,node)`); `TYPES` is still a live patchable object (the bridge lists it among the 11 monkey-patched symbols, and tests do `TYPES.blur.eval = …`).\n  8. Populate `writes` from the 17 `_`-prefixed fields 13 evals already produce, with `legacySlot` set to the exact dotted path the oracle reads. Nothing consumes any of it.\n     GATE: Digest 60/60 (nothing reads the new fields, so nothing can move) + `_verify_manifest.js` extended to assert: the set `{t : TYPES[t].exactUnderTransform}` is EXACTLY the 18 members of EXACT_TYPES (index.html:4048-4049); every param with unit 'cells⚠' has resolution === 'RESOLUTION_BOUND'; every declared `legacySlot` resolves on a freshly built node of that type. A8 · Arm the ch27 co-evolution assertion, and publish the debt ledger [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: definePlugin throws at registration when `process` is declared, `writes` contains 'height', and CO_EVOLUTION[process] ⊄ writes. The registry is now honest about a real, measured hole — ABSENT as ports today: soilDepth, sedimentDepth, sandDepth, wetness, moisture, flowVelocity, waterDepth. PRESENT already: temperature (encoded field + _temperatureC + FIELD_META), windVector (_wind.{u,v,speed}), snowDepth (_snowLayer.depthM with a closed volume/deposited/melted/moved budget), insolation (_solarExposure), a solar shadow. d_deposits/d_flow/d_wear/d_texture are ch27 DERIVED maps recomputed from final height — compliant, and must not be mistaken for state maps.\n     GATE: Digest 60/60 (nothing is computed) + new `_verify_coevolution.js`: registration of all 60 succeeds; the debt table prints `declared N / implemented M` per process; and a NEGATIVE test — a fixture plugin declaring process:'hydraulic' with writes:['height'] MUST throw. Without the negative test the assertion is decorative. This is the concrete cost of ch27-first on Phase A: one manifest concept, one validator, one gate file, zero lines of numeric code.\n  10. A9 · Multi-output `evaluate` behind a generated legacy `eval` shim (the highest-value step) [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: definePlugin accepts `evaluate(ctx) => Outputs` and GENERATES the legacy `eval` (the six-step shim in the contract). It exists ONLY because eval returns one Float32Array and has nowhere else to put what the sim computed: ch27's co-evolution rule failing in the concrete. Move it to `Outputs.budget` → `nd._budget`, keeping the global as a compatibility alias assigned by the shim, because `_verify_erosion_mass.js` reads it by name.\n     GATE: `_verify_erosion_mass.js` G1-G5c unchanged (G1 gpu-mass-closes ≤0.45, G2 cpu-mass-closes through the itemized ledger, G3 both engines write a self-consistent diag with the GPU's `exportedDerived:true` flagged, G4 settle-is-opt-in, G5a/b/c fans-and-no-beading) + digest 60/60 (the oracle does not digest hydroMassDiag, so this is free on the digest and gated entirely by the mass verifier — say that out loud rather than citing 60/60 as evidence).\n  12. A wrong unification therefore ships silently. Named substitutes: `_verify_realtime.js` (it monkey-patches gl.bindBuffer/bufferData and asserts WHICH named entries of `buffers` re-upload per interaction — 'SatMap recolours without rebuilding terrain geometry') and `_verify_build_progress.js`.
