A12 · Cut the framework-free core out of index.html into src/core/*, behind the bridge [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: Extraction order is FORCED by A4's import direction: `lattice-constants.ts` (no imports) → `document-state.ts` (terrainDef, RES, SCALE_RES, BUILD_QUALITY, XF) → `field.ts` (derived predicates + newField + sampleBilinear + resampleRect/resampleField + the ctx install) → `kernels/*` → `plugins/registry.ts` (the 60) → `graph.ts` (nodes/edges/the evaluators). The measurement that says this is viable: between index.html:1019 and :4667 there is EXACTLY ZERO DOM access — all CPU field kernels, all 60 TYPES entries and the graph model, 3,452 of ~7,180 script lines, are already framework-free. The 33 DOM calls inside GRAPH MODEL+eval (:4653-4899 — undo buttons, build HUD, stat bar) become an injected reporter; `finishGraphEvaluation` (:4817-4835, eight UI/GL calls) becomes an event emission. index.html keeps one `<script type=\"module\" src=\"src/legacy.js\">` and the bridge block is APPENDED after the app source (generate-bridge.mjs:32-35 explains why it cannot be an importable module: an accessor `() => RES` only reaches the real binding if it is lexically inside the declaring module).\n     GATE: `_verify_bridge.js --check` exits 0 (A1 made this checkable across all 66 verifiers) + digest 60/60 over BOTH `index.html` via the dev server AND `dist/index.html` (the production mode A1 had to un-break) + `_verify_shapescan.js` over the file LIST, not one file. That last clause is load-bearing: shapescan is the ONLY mechanical protection for the field-dimension conversion, it reads one hard-coded path (:20), and it will print PASS vacuously the instant index.html becomes a shell. `npm run verify` over http, because file:// cannot load a module bundle (run-legacy-verify.mjs exists exactly for this).\n  14. A13 · Make resampleRect the primary resampler and give the preview pyramid its own gate [digestSafe=true]\n     MOVES: `resampleField(f,fromN,toN)` allocates `new Float32Array(toN*toN)` and clamps rows to fromN-1 — square in, square out. `resampleRect(f,fromW,fromH,toW,toH)` (:2594-2602) is its rectangle-correct twin, written explicitly for this. Measured: 25 resampleField call sites vs 3 resampleRect. The square-only version is used on RECTANGULAR fields at evaluateSunShadowMap:3299,3303-3304; evaluateTemperatureMap:3308,3309,3324-3328; evaluateTerrainWind:3539-3540; evaluateSnowLayer:3791-3797,3801-3804 — and evaluateTemperatureMap is internally inconsistent, allocating at `preview*latticeRows(preview)` (:3314, rect-aware) then feeding resampleField (:3325-3326, square-only). Export resampleRect as primary; keep resampleField as a named square-in/square-out alias with the 25 sites listed as the un-migrated remainder. Change no call site in this step.\n     GATE: Digest 60/60 (no call site moves) — but the digest is NOT evidence here and must not be cited as such: all four sites are gated by `const full=RES,preview=BUILD_QUALITY===\"interactive\"?Math.min(full,512):full`, the oracle runs at RES=256, so `Math.min(256,512)===256`, preview===full, and the branch NEVER EXECUTES in the gate. So this step's real deliverable is a NEW gate: `_verify_preview_pyramid.js` at RES=1024, quality='interactive', both lattices, asserting every climate/wind/snow field has length `fieldLen()` and that the bottom ~15% of rows is not a zero band. Expect it RED on hex on arrival — that is a discovered defect, not a regression, and it must land as a named failing expectation with a ticket rather than be quietly fixed inside an 'extraction' commit.\nRISKS: AUX CANNOT RIDE ON `ins`. The oracle wires a DISTINCT perlin seed into every declared slot (_verify_digest.js:198-254, SEEDS={A:101,B:202,C:303,M:404,D:505}) specifically so a slot swap moves the digest. Any design that delivers ch27's typed aux ports by appending slots to `ins` moves the digest of every node it touches for zero behavioural reason. The contract must carry aux on a side channel — and a designer who has not read the WIRING table will not see this coming. | FIELD_META IS KEYED ON ARRAY OBJECT IDENTITY (index.html:3202, `new WeakMap()`). The A9 shim must return `evaluate()`'s exact Float32Array instance — any wrapper, copy, transferable or SharedArrayBuffer swap silently drops every physical-unit tag, and tempmask/d_heat/snow/water fall into their `_inputError` branches (:4461, :4505, :4592-4594). Six or more digests move at once, from a change that looks like plumbing. This is the single most fragile invariant in the file and it is declared nowhere in TYPES. | THE DIGEST IS STRUCTURALLY SQUARE-ONLY. `_verify_digest.js:354` SKIPS any node whose primary length !== RES*RES; on hex `fieldH()`=round(256·2/√3)=296≠256, so a hex sweep skips all sixty and reports `missing`. 60/60 is therefore not evidence about the lattice at all — and the three defects that live there (25 square-only resampleField sites, the simulateTerrainWind context leak at :3465, the GPU square-texture branch) all sit outside what it observes. | THE BRIDGE IS DEV/TEST-GATED. `src/testing/bridge-block.js:8` emits only under `import.meta.env.DEV || MODE==='test'`, so the documented production-artifact gate `node _verify_digest.js dist/index.html` (_verify_digest.js:12) dies the moment the bridge is the only test surface. Fix it in A1 or lose the ability to gate the thing you actually ship. | THE FROZEN BRIDGE SURFACE MISSES EXACTLY THE GATES THAT COVER THE DIGEST'S BLIND SPOTS. bridge-surface.json's fileList covers 56 of the 66 verifiers; 8 of the 10 uncovered are the lattice/hex gates. And of 21 field/lattice primitives only 4 are in the surface — fieldW, fieldH, fieldLen, latticeRows, isHex, HEX_ROW, HEX_NB_*, hexNb, HEX_EX/EY, resampleField, resampleRect, gAt, resScale, REF_RES, HEX_SQUARE_WORLD are all absent. Extracting field/lattice under today's bridge keeps the digest at 60/60 while silently blinding all eight hex gates: green digest, dead lattice coverage. | _verify_shapescan.js DIES QUIETLY AT THE FIRST EXTRACTION COMMIT. It reads ONE hard-coded file (`SHAPESCAN_FILE || '../../index.html'`, :20). When index.html becomes a shell no rule fires and it prints PASS. It is the ONLY mechanical protection for the field-dimension conversion, and the digest will not catch what it stops catching. | ADDING AN IMPLEMENTED AUX PORT CHANGES THE NODE'S DIGEST STRING even when the primary field is byte-identical, because the digest is a composite `parts.join('|')`. Without A2's component-aware comparator that is indistinguishable from drift, and the operational pressure will be to skip the port rather than re-baseline — which is exactly how ch27 gets retrofitted late, the failure mode the chapter exists to prevent. | `_snowLayer` IS A STRUCT, NOT A FIELD: one depth grid plus SEVEN float64 scalars the oracle pins individually (_verify_digest.js:271-275). An aux model admitting only Float32Array maps forces snow back into a node mutation and re-opens the hole the contract was built to close. Hence `scalarsOut` as a first-class port kind. | resolveColor's `n` PARAMETER IS A LIE. The in-code comment at index.html:5985-5987 concedes that colorErodeField calls priorityFloodFill(h), which reads the GLOBAL fieldW()/fieldH(), so 'callers must pass n === RES, which resolveColor guarantees'. A colour port whose signature promises a resolution parameter is promising something the implementation does not honour — declare it as ctx-resolution-bound or fix the coupling, but do not ship the false signature. | IDENTITY-KEYED CACHES MISS FOREVER UNDER A PURIFIED EVALUATOR. `_colorErosionRoute` compares `route.h===h` (:5978-5979), `_weatherSignal` compares `cached.h===h` (:6031-6032), `_biomeMaskCache` compares `hit.source===mask` (:6094-6096), and the viewport's whole geometry-rebuild decision is `curField!==f` / `curSnowLayerRef!==layerDepth` (:6819-6822). None is cleared by markDirtyFrom, restoreGraph or applyWorkingResolution — they self-invalidate purely because a re-eval produces a NEW array. If Phase B returns fresh arrays for unchanged inputs, colour erosion re-runs priority-flood plus a log2(steps) jump-pointer build and the viewport rebuilds its vertex/normal/index buffers every frame: a silent 5-50× regression the digest passes cleanly. Contract rule, stated normatively: identical cache key ⇒ identical array INSTANCE. | EXACT_TYPES MUST MOVE INTO THE MANIFEST IN PHASE A OR PLUGIN-PER-NODE IS DEFEATED. It is a hand-maintained Set of 18 type-id strings living outside the registry (index.html:4048-4049), consulted by exactChain (:4051-4056). Leave it and Phase B ships a hardcoded type-name list inside core/graph.ts. Related and unavoidable: `transform` (:4258-4269) is the one node that cannot be a pure (params, inputs, ctx) plugin — it reflects on the graph, mutates the ambient XF, and recursively re-evaluates its own upstream cone from inside its own eval. The contract needs `exactUnderTransform` plus `ctx.evaluateUpstream(slot,{exact})`; there is no version of this that is purely mechanical. | THE TWO DIRTY CLASSIFICATIONS DISAGREE BY CONSTRUCTION AND THE DIGEST CANNOT SEE IT. markParamDirty gates on `TYPES[nd.type].passthrough` (:4781, 7 types) while both evaluators carry `if(nd.type!==\"water\"&&nd.type!==\"snow\")colorDirty=true` (:4848 and :4883, 2 types). The oracle always evaluates from cold, so a wrong unification ships silently. Substitute gates: _verify_realtime.js (patches gl.bindBuffer/bufferData and asserts which named buffers re-upload) and _verify_build_progress.js — both must be run and their transcripts diffed, not merely 'green'. | A FUTURE NAMED RED, out of Phase A scope but it will land: _verify_realtime.js, _verify_render.js and _verify_satgen.js drive the app with `input.value = x; input.dispatchEvent(new Event('input'))` against `#pBody .field` located by matching label text to the TYPES param label. React installs a `_valueTracker` on controlled inputs, so a direct `.value` assignment makes React believe nothing changed and onChange never fires — three verifiers break even with byte-identical DOM. Either render inspector inputs uncontrolled with a ref plus a native listener, or the inspector commit rewrites those three drivers. _verify_digest.js is unaffected (it never touches the inspector), so 60/60 survives that commit — which is precisely why it must not be used as the evidence for it.",
    "verdictDigest": "### Digest-holding under the hard constraint: for each cut-list step, does byte-identity of 60 node outputs genuinely survive? Penalise digestSafe:true steps that REWRITE an expression rather than MOVE one; penalise steps too large to bisect a drift to a cause; penalise any weakening of the oracle, since a loosened instrument converts \"60/60 green\" from evidence into a tautology.\nRANKING: DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A > DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited > DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile)\n  DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A: 8/10 - Best step-size discipline of the three, and the only design that measured a blocker I could reproduce. Its A1 claims `_verify_bridge.js --check` \"currently exits FATAL — acorn is not installed\". I ran it: it prints the FATAL banner and exits 2. Designs 1 and 3 both prescribe that exact command as a gate without noticing it cannot run. A1 also correctly notes package.json has no `bridge:gen` (confirmed: scripts are dev/preview/build/verify/test:e2e/test:e2e:ui) despite generate-bridge.mjs:56 telling you to run it.\n\nOn bisectability it is the clear winner. A7 lands the contract and \"register[s] ZERO nodes through them\", with _verify_contract.js proving the contract is \"expressible against the existing 60 before a single node moves\". A12 then pilots exactly TWO nodes, and the selection rationale is the strongest single sentence in any of the three cut lists: \"Pick these two because the digest already hashes their aux by dotted path, so the reproduction is proven by the gate rather than by inspection.\" I confirmed the oracle's AUX table hardcodes d_wind:['_wind.u','_wind.v','_wind.speed'] and d_sunshadow:['_solarExposure'] — so a mis-scatter in the adapter fails immediately and names one of two suspects. Compare Design 3's A6, which converts all 60 entries in one commit.\n\nIt is also the only design that states the constraint definePlugin's output must satisfy to survive the OTHER gates: \"A PLAIN, WRITABLE, OWN DATA PROPERTY. Not a getter, not frozen, not a Proxy\" because _verify_build_progress.js monkey-patches TYPES.blur.eval. Design 1 says nothing about this and its `{...spec}` spread plus generated `eval` closure would satisfy it only by accident.\n\nHonesty is good where it matters: rootSeed ships as an identity derivation with the reason given (\"a contract that quietly claimed per-node seeded RNG while shipping identity would be the same category of lie as the inert `effect` key\"), and it verified the premise for that — I confirmed zero `.effect` read sites in 8,112 lines.\n\nFaults. (a) It miscounts EXACT_TYPES: A8 says \"the 18 types currently hardcoded in the Set at index.html:4048-4049\" and then lists 19 names. I counted the literal: 19. A gate that asserts \"the computed set equals the 18-member literal\" fails on arrival. (b) Its Phase A stops short of the brief's goal: A14 extracts only lattice-constants/document/field, leaving kernels, the registry and graph.ts in index.html. (c) A11 adds mandatory `unit` and `invalidates` to all 235 param declarations in one commit — inert, so digest-safe, but a very large diff to review for a step whose value is entirely deferred.\n  DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited: 7/10 - States the governing digest-safety principle better than either rival, and the principle is the right one: \"THE ORACLE FILE IS NOT EDITED IN ANY PHASE A COMMIT. That is the design goal, not a nicety: the moment the oracle and the app move in the same commit, '60/60 green' stops being evidence.\" That sentence is the correct reading of the hard constraint, and it is precisely what Design 3 violates in its second commit.\n\nIts freeness argument is measured and I confirmed it: \"ZERO occurrences of Object.keys(def), Object.entries(def), `for (... in def)`, or JSON.stringify(def) over a TYPES entry\". Grep finds only Object.keys(TYPES) at :5287 and :5338 — over the registry, not an entry. So \"an unknown key on a TYPES entry is UNOBSERVABLE by construction\" holds, and the whole additive-manifest tranche (step 7) is genuinely free. It also gets EXACT_TYPES right where it counts — step 7's assertion says \"(19 ids)\", which matches the literal I counted — though its own contract comment two pages earlier says \"18 strings\" and then lists 19. In a design that opens by insisting numbers be measured, that is a self-inflicted wound.\n\nStep sizing is good on the one step that could go badly: step 10 migrates the ~36 pure evals \"in batches of ~8 so a drift names at most 8 suspects\", and it forbids the thing that would actually move a digest — \"Do NOT touch the expression itself — no re-association, no hoisting, no ctx.newField() substitution yet\". That is the correct discipline; it is a move, not a rewrite.\n\nBest-in-class on labelling green-without-evidence. Step 9's gate reads \"THE DIGEST IS GREEN HERE AND IS NOT EVIDENCE — say so in the commit message\", with _verify_realtime.js/_verify_build_progress.js/a new _verify_dirty.js named as substitutes. I confirmed the asymmetry it is worried about: `passthrough` has exactly one read site (:4781) covering 7 types, and the evaluators carry a separate 2-type rule.\n\nFaults, and they are what drop it below Design 2. (a) It does not deliver the brief's Phase A. The goal is \"extract a framework-free core\"; the only file that leaves index.html in 14 steps is src/core/context.ts (step 6). Step 4 is explicitly \"pure motion, one file\". Field, lattice, kernels, registry and graph all stay inline. Design 2 and 3 both actually cut. (b) Its ports are keyed by label — `bindPorts(spec, ins)` \"positional -> named\", and the fallback is `ports[spec.ins[0]]`. That is sound only if slot labels are unique per node. I measured every ins array and no node has a duplicate today, so it works — but `assertPortsMatchIns` as specified checks order and length, not uniqueness, so the invariant that makes the scheme safe is the one it does not assert. Design 3's index-based ctx.input(slot) has no such exposure. (c) TYPES is rebuilt as `Object.fromEntries(PLUGINS.map(...))`; Object.keys(TYPES) drives the node-menu build at :5287 and the count at :5338, so registry insertion order becomes load-bearing for UI and is never mentioned.\n  DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile): 5/10 - Has the best individual insights and the best answer to the stated Phase A goal, but it is the only design that weakens the oracle, and it does so in its second commit, before any code moves.\n\nA2 is the disqualifying step under this lens. It rewrites the comparator so that \"component ADDED = REVIEW + explicit re-baseline\" instead of a failure — while the design itself concedes, in coEvolution, \"No auxiliary map is actually COMPUTED in Phase A.\" So the loosening buys nothing in Phase A and is pure instrument decay ahead of need. Worse is the second half: \"derive the `AUX`/`SCALARS` tables (currently hardcoded at :259-275) from `TYPES[t].writes`/`scalarsOut`\". I read those tables. They are hardcoded IN THE ORACLE precisely so the oracle knows independently that d_wind must expose _wind.u/_wind.v/_wind.speed and that snow must expose seven named scalars. Deriving them from the app's own manifest inverts the trust direction: a plugin that loses a `writes` entry during Phase B silently deletes its own coverage and the gate prints PASS. That is exactly the \"oracle went blind\" failure the design quotes _verify_digest.js's header about — proposed here as digestSafe:true. Design 1's rule (\"the oracle file is not edited in any Phase A commit\") exists to prevent this specific move.\n\nSecond fault: the two largest commits are unbatched. A6 converts \"`const TYPES={…}` (index.html:4068-4651) into a registry of 60 `definePlugin({…})` calls\" in one step — 60 suspects on a drift. A12 then moves lattice-constants, document-state, field, kernels/*, the 60-plugin registry AND graph.ts out of index.html in a single commit; a drift there is bisectable only to \"the core\". Design 1 batches its equivalent at ~8 and Design 2 pilots at 2.\n\nWhat is genuinely excellent and should be grafted. The sharpest constraint-derivation in any of the three: \"AUX CANNOT RIDE ON `ins`. The oracle wires a DISTINCT perlin seed into every declared slot ... specifically so a slot swap moves the digest.\" That is a real property of the gate turned into a contract shape, and its side-channel ctx.inputAux(slot, name) with index-based ctx.input(slot) is the safest port model proposed. Its A4 extraction-order fix (\"constants ← nothing, state ← nothing, derived ← both, kernels ← derived\") is the only one that explains WHY the order is forced and correctly notes the failure mode is \"ReferenceErrors, not wrong numbers\", gated via the oracle's pageerror channel. And A13 is exactly the honesty the brief asked for: a NEW gate \"Expect it RED on hex on arrival — that is a discovered defect, not a regression\", named rather than smuggled.\n\nAlso miscounts EXACT_TYPES as \"the 18 members\" (A7). I counted the literal at :4048-4049: 19. A7's assertion would fail as written.\n\nOn the shared claims it is accurate: I verified erosionScale defaults to .38 at :4383, featureScale = lerp(1,7,.38) = 3.28 > the `if(!(k>1.02))` guard, and Math.round(256/3.28) = 78 — so its \"RES rebound to 78 at the digest's 256\" is exactly right and its A5 gate is genuinely sensitive rather than lucky.\nGRAFT: D1's governing invariant, adopted verbatim as the Phase A rule: the oracle file is not edited in any Phase A commit. It is the only rule that keeps 60/60 as evidence rather than a tautology, and it is what forbids D3's A2. | D2's A1, promoted to commit zero: `npm i acorn` + a `bridge:gen` script. I ran `node tests/legacy/_verify_bridge.js --check` — it prints FATAL and exits 2 today. D1 step 1 and D3 A1 both name this command as their gate; neither can run it. | D2's A12 pilot-selection rule: migrate d_wind and d_sunshadow FIRST because the oracle already hashes their aux by dotted path (AUX table, hardcoded: d_wind:['_wind.u','_wind.v','_wind.speed'], d_sunshadow:['_solarExposure']), so the adapter's scatter is proven by the gate rather than by inspection. Then batch the rest at D1's ~8 per commit. | D3's derivation that aux maps CANNOT ride on `ins` — the oracle wires a distinct seed per slot so a slot swap moves the digest — plus its index-based `ctx.input(slot)` / `ctx.inputAux(slot, name)`. Index-keying also removes the unstated label-uniqueness invariant D1 and D2 depend on (I verified no node has duplicate ins labels today, so it is a latent, not live, hazard). | D3's A4 import-direction fix and forced extraction order (lattice-constants → document-state → derived predicates → kernels → registry → graph), including its gate insight that a TDZ break surfaces on the oracle's pageerror channel as a ReferenceError, not as a wrong number. | D2's requirement that the generated `eval` be a plain writable own DATA property — not a getter, Proxy or frozen — because _verify_build_progress.js monkey-patches TYPES.blur.eval and bridge-surface.json records TYPES as patched. | D1's explicit labelling of green-without-evidence steps (dirty-propagation, hydroMassDiag, preview pyramid) with named substitute gates, and its instruction to say so in the commit message rather than citing 60/60. | D3's A13 posture for the preview-pyramid gate: land it as a named failing expectation expected RED on hex, as a discovered defect with a ticket, rather than quietly fixing it inside an extraction commit. | D1's measured freeness argument for additive manifest fields (I confirmed zero Object.keys/entries/for-in/JSON.stringify over a TYPES entry; only Object.keys(TYPES) over the registry at :5287 and :5338), plus a standing scan asserting no new entry enumeration appears.\nFATAL: DESIGN 3 A2, first half: downgrading a digest component from FAIL to REVIEW before Phase A computes a single auxiliary map. The design itself states 'No auxiliary map is actually COMPUTED in Phase A', so this is instrument decay with no Phase A benefit. | DESIGN 3 A2, second half: deriving the oracle's AUX/SCALARS tables from `TYPES[t].writes`/`scalarsOut`. Those tables are hardcoded inside _verify_digest.js so the oracle independently knows what each node must expose. Sourcing them from the app makes the gate self-referential — dropping a `writes` entry silently deletes its own coverage and the gate prints PASS. This is the 'oracle goes blind' failure the design quotes the oracle's own header about. | DESIGN 3 A6 and A12 are unbatched mega-commits (all 60 registry entries in one; the entire core — constants, state, field, kernels, registry, graph — in one). A drift in either is bisectable only to 'the core', which the lens explicitly flags. | MEASUREMENT FAILURE, D2 (A8) and D3 (A7): both call EXACT_TYPES an 18-member set. The literal at index.html:4048-4049 contains 19 strings (perlin simplex ridged worley gradient shape drawmask constant levels curve clampn invert terrace blend add maxmin smin smax stampn). Both designs' assertions — 'the computed set equals the 18-member literal' — fail on arrival. D1 gets it right in its gate (19) but says 18 in its contract prose. | SCOPE GAP, D1: the brief's Phase A is 'extract a framework-free core', but across 14 steps the only file leaving index.html is src/core/context.ts. Step 4 is explicitly 'pure motion, one file'. The contract is the best-argued of the three; the extraction is essentially not attempted.\n\n### Does it satisfy chapter 27 — genuine enforcement of the co-evolution rule (registry-time check, not a comment), a respected state-vs-derived split, and auxiliary maps as mandatory first-class ports rather than optional metadata?\nRANKING: DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A > DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile) > DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited\n  DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A: 8.5/10 - The only design that enforces ch27 at the three places the chapter actually demands. (1) Registry-time: LAYER 2 is a hard throw — 'definePlugin asserts OBLIGATIONS[process] ⊆ outPorts.kind ∪ keys(coEvolutionDebt) and throws otherwise... For the 61st node written by a third party this is an absolute wall.' That is the lens's preferred mechanism, present and non-optional. (2) The ratchet is the strictest of the three: 'Debt may shrink, never grow' — a new non-compliant node cannot ship even with a declared excuse, where DESIGN 1 permits adding a debt entry. (3) It is the ONLY design that carries ch27's manifest staleness contract into the type system: PortDesc.derivedFrom, annotated 'Feeds 27's manifest parent-hash rule (\"an engine that loads curvature whose parent hash does not match height's is loading a lie\")', with LAYER 5 making derived ports inherit the parent's cache key. DESIGN 1 omits parent hashing entirely; DESIGN 3 names ch14's Merkle key but carries no field for it.\n\nIt is also the only one that ASSERTS the state/derived split rather than declaring it: 'A plugin whose only inPort kind is height and whose writes is empty may not emit a state port — that shape is definitionally \"recomputed from final geometry\", which 27 forbids for state maps.' And it classifies correctly where DESIGN 1 does not: it puts d_sunshadow on 'the derived side... recomputed from final height, which is what 27 requires of derived maps', matching ch27's verbatim derived list (curvature, ao, slope, aspect, insolation, TWI).\n\nAux maps are mandatory, not metadata: outPorts and writes carry no `?`, and evaluate returns `ports: Record<string, Float32Array>` — a non-optional record keyed by declared labels. LAYER 6 blocks the back door ('a node cannot quietly reach for soilDepth: it declares an inPort of kind soilDepth and the graph supplies it or the node does not run'). Registry vocabulary is the most complete of the three (includes strataHardness, slope, aspect), and it respects ch27 §Naming by measuring that 8 of the names occur zero times in index.html — 'new vocabulary, not renames of anything'.\n\nFaults, and they are real. It is SILENT on the Masking Doctrine: 'albedo' is listed as a plain app-native FieldKind and resolveAlbedo as 'the declared albedo output port', with no statement anywhere that baked colour is prohibited from the runtime handoff and survives only as a preview/review product. ch27 calls that prohibition 'absolute' and 'the whole point of the handoff'; DESIGN 1 gets it right and this design does not engage it. Its MassBudget ({in,out,settled,exported,lost,named}) lacks ch27's explicit stack semantics — the chapter mechanises the check as assert_layer_budget, 'which sums the stack, not the layers separately — the layers legitimately exchange mass'. And its aux-consumption model ('declares an inPort of kind soilDepth') collides with the oracle: the WIRING/SEEDS tables are hardcoded per type, so a new aux inPort is either permanently null under the gate or forces an oracle edit — the fork DESIGN 3 caught and this one did not. It also permits sourcing height from node A and soilDepth from node B, which is the retrofit shape ch27 warns against.\n  DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile): 7/10 - Best physical modelling of ch27's verification hooks, undercut by two failures the lens names explicitly.\n\nStrengths are substantial and specific. Its MassBudget is the closest of the three to ch27 §Verification: it carries `stack: AuxName[]` annotated '27: sum the STACK, not the layers separately — layers legitimately exchange mass', plus `derived?: boolean` for the GPU path that infers exported by elimination. ScalarPort is a first-class port kind, correctly justified: '_snowLayer IS A STRUCT, NOT A FIELD: one depth grid plus SEVEN float64 scalars the oracle pins individually... An aux model admitting only Float32Array maps forces snow back into a node mutation.' I confirmed those seven scalars at _verify_digest.js:270-275. Its `encoding?: {kind:'temperature'; minC; maxC}` is the only engagement with ch27's golden precision rule against the app's real [0,1] Celsius packing. It types StateMap/VectorMap/DerivedMap as separate unions ('OPPOSITE lifecycles and therefore must be different types, not a flag on one type') and gets insolation on the derived side, matching ch27. The registration wall comes with a negative test, and the justification is the sharpest sentence any design offers on the lens's own question: 'Without the negative test the assertion is decorative.'\n\nAnd its A2 is the single most valuable ch27 insight in the whole set, which I verified independently: the node digest is a composite `parts.join('|')` (_verify_digest.js:411) with named components, and AUX/SCALARS are hardcoded tables (:258-275). So the first genuinely implemented auxiliary map appends a component and reads as drift under a whole-string comparator, while remaining uncovered unless the tables are hand-edited. A2 makes the comparator component-aware ('component ADDED = REVIEW + explicit re-baseline') and derives AUX/SCALARS from TYPES[t].writes. Its own diagnosis is correct: 'the operational pressure will be to skip the port rather than re-baseline — which is exactly how ch27 gets retrofitted late, the failure mode the chapter exists to prevent.' Neither other design noticed that shipping ch27 and holding the gate are structurally in tension.\n\nBut: `writes?: AuxPort[]`, `reads?`, `scalarsOut?` are all OPTIONAL, and Outputs.aux is `Partial<Record<AuxName, Float32Array>>`. That is auxiliary maps as optional metadata in the type system — the exact shape the lens says to penalise, and the only design of the three with the `?`. The registration wall fires only when `process` is declared, so a node that writes height and declares nothing is legal by construction. Worse, the declared escape is inside `writes` itself — A8 says 'add the required port names to writes marked implemented:false' — which satisfies the boot throw for free, and the companion gate only PRINTS: 'the debt table prints declared N / implemented M per process'. There is no fail-on-growth ratchet. A printed ledger is nearer a comment than a check, which is the distinction this lens exists to make. Two further ch27 errors: A8 instructs 'declare process on the seven nodes ch27's table names (... snow, weathering)', but this app's weathering node is a colour effect that paints rock stains, not the Heimsath soil-production producer — DESIGN 1 caught this trap and this design walks into it. And LAYER 3's rule that 'a kind:derived port may NOT appear in any downstream node's reads' would forbid mask nodes consuming d_curvature/d_slope, which the existing 60-node graph does constantly; ch27 requires derived maps be recomputed rather than patched, not that they be unreadable.\n  DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited: 6.5/10 - Has the strongest single runtime invariant and the only correct reading of the Masking Doctrine, but the ch27 obligation itself is checked in the weakest place of the three, and it commits the one classification error ch27 singles out.\n\nWhat it gets right, and no one else does: it is the only design that engages ch27's colour prohibition. MapName carries albedo annotated 'PREVIEW ONLY — Doctrine forbids baked colour in the runtime handoff; it survives as a review product only', and step 13 declares `writes:[{map:'albedo', kind:'preview'}]` with the reason spelled out. ch27 calls that separation 'absolute' and 'the whole point of the handoff'; DESIGN 2 and DESIGN 3 are both silent on it. It is also the only one to catch a live trap in the obligation table: 'this app's weathering node is a COLOUR effect, not ch27's weathering/soil-production process (the Heimsath production function). It must declare process: undefined... or the ratchet will demand soilDepth from a node that paints rock stains.' DESIGN 3 makes exactly that mistake. Its assertWritesExact — 'Missing => throw. Extra => throw' — closes a leak the other two leave open, since neither asserts against undeclared extras, and its justification is the right one: 'There is no longer anywhere to put a computed quantity except a declared port', citing hydroMassDiag as the concrete failure. `writes` is mandatory. The ratchet is bidirectional (fails if a debt entry is fixed but not removed), which keeps the ledger honest against reality.\n\nWhere it falls down on this lens. The runtime throw enforces only declaration↔return self-consistency; the ch27 obligation itself is 'SIDE B — THE STATIC ch27 RATCHET... a new gate, tests/legacy/_verify_coevolution.js', a CI file that 'reads the manifest and never runs the app'. That is enforcement, but it is the weakest siting of the three — the lens asks specifically for a registry-time check, and DESIGN 2 and DESIGN 3 both throw at registration where this one waits for CI. Worse, it misfiles the state/derived split at exactly the point ch27 warns about: under the heading 'ALREADY ch27-COMPLIANT STATE PORTS' it lists 'insolation — d_sunshadow/_solarExposure... Matches.' ch27's derived list is verbatim 'curvature, ao, slope, aspect, insolation, TWI', and the chapter frames conflating the two kinds as 'this chapter's version of the effect-mask/process-mask confusion'. Filing insolation as state attaches the wrong lifecycle to it — carried through and never recomputed, when ch27 requires it be recomputed after the last height write and never patched. It also drops ch27's manifest layer table and the derived-map parent content hash entirely, so the staleness contract the chapter makes checkable engine-side has no representation. And having correctly identified the mass budget as ch27's live failure ('ch27 §Verification requires a closed budget for every conserved quantity; this is that requirement failing in code'), it then models it as `diagnostics?: Record<string, number>` — an untyped number bag with no stack semantics, where ch27 names the mechanised assert that sums the stack. Its legacyOpaque escape for _wind and _snowLayer is honest but means the two vector/struct maps are not typed ports in Phase A, deferring the First-Class Citizen Rule for precisely the maps ch27 specifies as RG32F.\nGRAFT: DESIGN 3's A2 component-aware digest comparator, into whichever design wins — this is not optional polish. Verified independently: the node digest is `parts.join('|')` (_verify_digest.js:411) with named components, AUX is a hardcoded 7-type table (:258-267) and SCALARS a hardcoded 7-scalar snow table (:270-275). So (a) the first genuinely implemented auxiliary map appends a component and reads as drift under a whole-string compare, and (b) it is uncovered until someone hand-edits the tables. Take both halves: parse the composite into per-component verdicts (primary drifted = FAIL, component removed = FAIL, component ADDED = REVIEW + explicit re-baseline), and derive AUX/SCALARS from TYPES[t].writes/scalarsOut so new ports auto-extend coverage. Without this, DESIGN 2's whole Phase B creates standing pressure to skip the port. | DESIGN 3's MassBudget shape into DESIGN 2's LAYER 4: `stack: AuxName[]` plus `derived?: boolean`. ch27 §Verification mechanises the check as assert_layer_budget, 'which sums the stack, not the layers separately — the layers legitimately exchange mass'. DESIGN 2's {in,out,settled,exported,lost,named} cannot express that, and the `derived` flag is needed because the GPU pipe path infers `exported` by elimination (_verify_erosion_mass.js G3 already depends on knowing this). | DESIGN 3's ScalarPort as a first-class port kind. snow's seven float64s are digested individually and confirmed at _verify_digest.js:270-275; an aux model admitting only Float32Array forces snow back into node mutation and re-opens the hole the contract exists to close. | DESIGN 1's Masking Doctrine annotation, wholesale. albedo must be `kind:'preview'`, explicitly excluded from the runtime handoff, with ch27's parenthetical carried into the contract text ('the satmap/colour-map emitter of 08 survives as a preview and review product... but it is not part of the runtime handoff, and no engine-side system may depend on it'). DESIGN 2 and DESIGN 3 both ship an albedo/colour port with no such constraint, which is the one prohibition ch27 calls absolute. | DESIGN 1's weathering correction into the obligation table: this app's `weathering` node paints rock stains and must declare `process: undefined` with `writes:[{map:'albedo', kind:'preview'}]`. DESIGN 3's A8 explicitly instructs the opposite and would demand soilDepth from a colour node. | DESIGN 1's assertWritesExact (missing OR extra throws). DESIGN 2's LAYER 1 structural argument stops a node computing aux in a later pass but does not stop it returning an undeclared map; the exact-key-set assertion closes that and costs nothing. | DESIGN 3's negative-test requirement for the registration wall — a fixture plugin declaring process:'hydraulic' with writes:['height'] MUST throw, asserted in CI. 'Without the negative test the assertion is decorative' is the correct standard for this lens and DESIGN 2 does not state it. | DESIGN 3's finding that aux cannot ride on `ins` — the oracle wires a distinct seed per declared slot from a hardcoded WIRING table, so DESIGN 2's 'declares an inPort of kind soilDepth' model yields either a permanently-null slot under the gate or a forced oracle edit. Reconcile before Phase B: either bundle aux to the primary edge (DESIGN 3's ctx.inputAux(slot, name), which additionally makes it impossible to source height from node A and soilDepth from node B) or extend WIRING deliberately as an oracle change. | DESIGN 1's legacyOpaque escape for `_wind` and `_snowLayer` — a pragmatic, time-boxed Phase A concession for the two struct-shaped legacy slots, better than pretending they are already RG32F ports.\nFATAL: Chapter 27 is not in this working tree and every obligation table in all three designs was transcribed from elsewhere. Verified: terrain-architect/references/ holds 00-26 + 99-papers.md + open-source-grounding.json; the chapter exists only on origin/main (commits db31a68 'Add engine-data-handoff chapter formalising first-class auxiliary maps' and 1ed813c). All three name this as step zero, correctly — but until the rebase lands, CO_EVOLUTION / CH27_REQUIRES / OBLIGATIONS are three independent hand-transcriptions of a document nobody on this branch can open, and they already disagree with each other on `weathering`. | insolation is a DERIVED map. ch27 §State maps vs derived maps, verbatim: 'Derived maps are pure functions of the final fields: curvature, ao, slope, aspect, insolation, TWI.' DESIGN 1 files it under 'ALREADY ch27-COMPLIANT STATE PORTS' and calls it a match. That attaches state-map lifecycle rules (carried through, never recomputed) to a map ch27 requires be recomputed after the last height write and never patched — the precise conflation the chapter calls 'this chapter's version of the effect-mask/process-mask confusion'. DESIGN 2 and DESIGN 3 both get it right; DESIGN 1 must be corrected before its ratchet baseline is frozen, or the debt file encodes the error. | No design splits the `wetness` hybrid, which ch27 requires explicitly: 'Some maps are hybrids and must be split explicitly: wetness has a state component... and a derived component (TWI)... Ship them as what they are — the state map is evidence, the derived map is a prediction — and let the engine blend them; do not average them in the tool and lose both meanings.' All three carry a single `wetness` name. DESIGN 3 is worst placed to fix it later: its disjoint StateMap/VectorMap/DerivedMap unions make a wetness-with-both-lifecycles unrepresentable, and TWI appears in no union at all. DESIGN 2's per-port lifecycle flag is the only structure that can absorb the fix without a type-level rewrite. | Two of ch27's four verification hooks are unaddressed by all three designs. The co-evolution budget is covered (all three repoint _verify_erosion_mass.js). Missing entirely: the staleness check ('Recompute every derived map from the final R32F fields and diff against the export; any mismatch means a patched or pre-final-geometry map escaped'), and the snow audit ('every cell with snowDepth > 0 and moisture ≈ 0 is attributable to wind-loading, an avalanche runout, or glacial transport'). DESIGN 2's derivedFrom is the only field that would make the first one mechanisable; nothing in any design supports the second, and both DESIGN 1 and DESIGN 2 correctly measure that the Snow Rule is currently unsatisfiable because no node produces `moisture`. | DESIGN 3 treats auxiliary maps as optional metadata at the type level — `writes?: AuxPort[]`, `reads?`, `scalarsOut?`, and `Outputs.aux?: Partial<Record<...>>`. Combined with a registration wall that fires only when `process` is declared, and a debt escape (`writes` entries marked `implemented:false`) policed by a gate that merely 'prints declared N / implemented M per process' with no fail-on-growth ratchet, the enforcement is materially weaker than its five-layer framing claims. Fix by making `writes` mandatory and adding DESIGN 2's monotone baseline diff before this design is used as the basis for anything. | DESIGN 3's LAYER 3 rule — 'a kind:derived port may NOT appear in any downstream node's reads' — is over-strict and unreconciled with the existing graph. ch27 requires derived maps be recomputed rather than patched, not that they be unreadable; the app's mask nodes consume d_curvature/d_slope routinely, and ch27's own engine-consumer table lists curvature/ao/slope as things downstream systems read. As written this rule would either be silently ignored or break the 60-node library.\n\n### COST OF PHASE B, MEASURED IN NODE-TOUCHES. I scored each design by three numbers it must pay: (a) how many of the 60 nodes need a real rewrite versus a mechanical move, and how many are touched more than once across Phase A; (b) how many hidden globals must actually be threaded (all three converge on the same 4 mutable + 4 quasi-const, so this stopped discriminating and I weighted per-node API ceremony instead — how many mandatory fields each contract forces per plugin and per param); (c) whether Phase B's 60 units can be done by disjoint agents on disjoint files, or whether something in the design forces serial review. The decisive sub-question under (c) turned out not to be file layout — all three land a barrel registry that parallelises fine — but the ORACLE: implementing an auxiliary map necessarily appends a component to the composite digest string (`parts.join('|')`, _verify_digest.js:411), so unless the comparator can distinguish \"component added\" from \"primary drifted\", every Phase B/C aux implementation is a blind `--write` that a human must review serially, one node at a time. That is where the 60 units actually get bottlenecked, and only one design addresses it.\nRANKING: DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile) > DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A > DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited\n  DESIGN 3: Phase A — the installed-context core and the `definePlugin` contract (ch27-first, digest-immobile): 8/10 - CHEAPEST PHASE B, because it is the only design that removes the two things that would otherwise make Phase B serial.\n\nFIRST: A2, the component-aware digest comparator. It alone notices that a node's digest is `parts.join('|')` (_verify_digest.js:411) and that implementing soilDepth on hydraulic therefore changes the string even when the primary field is byte-identical. Its verdict split — `primary drifted`=FAIL, `component removed`=FAIL, `component ADDED`=REVIEW+re-baseline — plus `--component=primary`, is what lets N agents implement N aux maps concurrently and still prove each one left the height field untouched. Design 2's answer to the same situation is 'each moving that node's digest and needing its own `--write`', i.e. a full-node re-baseline that discards the primary guarantee; Design 1 never confronts it at all (its aux step only RELOCATES existing `_*` fields, which is genuinely byte-neutral, so the problem is invisible until Phase C). A2 also comes with a mutation drill — 'perturb one kernel constant in a scratch copy and assert EXACTLY ONE component of ONE node moves' — so the new comparator is proven sensitive rather than merely quiet. That is the single highest-leverage step in any of the three plans.\n\nSECOND: aux does not ride on `ins`. It is the only design that traces the mechanism — 'The oracle wires a DISTINCT perlin seed into every declared slot (_verify_digest.js:198-254) specifically so a slot swap moves the digest' — and concludes ch27's typed ports must arrive as `ctx.inputAux(slot, name)` with `ins` order 'frozen permanently'. It states the cost honestly ('That is not a compromise of ch27... but it is a real constraint the hard gate imposes'). Design 2 takes the opposite route on the same page count and never notices the cost.\n\nCEREMONY: lightest of the three. Every new ParamDesc field is optional (`unit?`, `invalidates?`, `quantise?`, `promotableToField?`), against Design 2's mandatory `unit` + `invalidates` on all 235 declarations. Eleven mandatory PluginDesc fields versus Design 2's seventeen. It declares `variadic` for colormixer and adds `'layerStack'`/`'strokes'` ParamDesc types for the two nodes where `def.params` is incomplete — closing the exact hole Design 1 names and then leaves open.\n\nFAULTS. One is structural and embarrassing for a contract document: `evaluate(ctx: EvalContext): Outputs` is the single most-used signature in the design, times 60, and the EvalContext interface as written has **no `params` member**. A plugin cannot read its own parameters. The design even declares `scalingPolicy?: (p: Params, ctx: EvalContext) => Params`, referencing a `Params` type that the eval entry point never receives. One line to fix, but it is the core signature.\n\nSecond: it takes the double-touch penalty twice over. A6 wraps all 60 in pass-through `definePlugin` calls, A7 adds manifest fields to all 60, A9 rewrites 13 bodies — two full passes over the registry plus a third over the hard thirteen, separated only for digest hygiene. A6 and A7 should be one commit.\n\nThird: it asserts `{t : TYPES[t].exactUnderTransform}` is 'EXACTLY the 18 members of EXACT_TYPES (index.html:4048-4049)'. I read the literal: it has 19. That gate fails on arrival — which is, ironically, precisely the argument for moving the hand-maintained Set into the manifest.\n\nFourth: 26 members on EvalContext is the largest API surface a plugin author must learn, and `ctx.rng`/`ctx.rootSeed`/`ctx.ledger`/`ctx.progress` ship unused. The design says so plainly ('this is ADDITIVE AND UNUSED'), which is honest, but it is API paid for and not consumed.\n\nWhat it gets right that neither rival does: A9 migrates the THIRTEEN HARDEST nodes in Phase A, so the contract is validated against transform-adjacent, aux-writing, scalar-returning cases before 47 easy ones are farmed out in parallel. That is the correct risk ordering.\n  DESIGN 2: Adapter-First Plugin Contract (definePlugin → legacy registry entry), Phase A: 6.5/10 - LIGHTEST PHASE A, HEAVIEST PARAM TAX, AND IT BETS THE CONTRACT ON A SAMPLE OF TWO.\n\nCREDIT WHERE IT IS DUE AND VERIFIED: A1 is the only step in any of the three plans that noticed the instrument is not merely stale but INOPERABLE. I checked: `acorn` is in neither `package.json` devDependencies nor `node_modules`, and `_verify_bridge.js:33-44` refuses a regex fallback and exits FATAL. Design 1's step 1 gate ('`node _verify_bridge.js --check` must report zero uncovered symbols') and Design 3's A1 gate ('exits 0') are both commands that cannot run today. Design 2 also measured the fileList staleness as 'STALE BY EXACTLY 10' and enumerated them, versus the survey's 11 — its counts are the most carefully re-derived of the three.\n\nCOST ANALYSIS. Phase A double-touch is genuinely the lowest: A11 adds manifest to all 60 in one pass, A12 pilots two nodes. Every other node is touched exactly once more, in Phase B, when it moves. On the lens's stated penalty ('touching all 60 nodes twice') this is the winner.\n\nBut it pays for that three times over elsewhere.\n\n(1) THE PARAM TAX. `ParamDesc` makes `unit` and `invalidates` MANDATORY. There are 235 param declarations across 60 nodes. That is ~470 forced judgements, landed in a single commit (A11), and `invalidates: 'VALUE'|'TOPOLOGY'|'COMPOSE_ONLY'` is not mechanical — the design's own justification derives COMPOSE_ONLY from canyon's exclusion of `depth` from its cache key, which required reading canyonComposeField to understand. Getting 470 of those wrong is silent: nothing reads the field in Phase A. Design 3 makes the identical fields optional and loses nothing.\n\n(2) IT COMMITS PHASE B/C TO THE EXPENSIVE AUX ROUTE. 'A node that needs soilDepth declares an inPort of kind soilDepth, and the graph either supplies it or the node does not run.' Adding an inPort changes `nodeInputs(nd).length`, which the oracle deliberately made digest-visible by seeding each slot distinctly, AND saved graphs persist `edge.slot` (colormixer's reorder rewrites `edges[].slot` in place at :5573-5576). So every erosion node that gains a soilDepth input costs a `--write` re-baseline plus a saved-graph slot migration. The design acknowledges the re-baseline but attributes it to the new map rather than to the slot mechanism, and never considers a side channel. That is the largest avoidable cost in the plan and it lands squarely in Phase B.\n\n(3) THE CONTRACT IS VALIDATED AGAINST 2 OF 60. A7's `_verify_contract.js` proves the LEGACY shape is expressible over the existing 60, which is a static property; A12 exercises `evaluate()` on d_wind and d_sunshadow. Then 58 nodes get extracted in parallel against a contract with a sample size of two. A hole found at node 30 re-touches 29. Design 3 validates against the thirteen hardest first; Design 1 against forty-nine. This is the exact inverse of the risk ordering the lens rewards, and 'parallel' is worth less when it is parallel-and-unvalidated.\n\n(4) SELF-INFLICTED FUTURE RE-TOUCHES. Two identity fields (`typeId: 'erosion.hydraulic/1'` plus `legacyId: 'hydraulic'`) with the rename deferred to Phase C. And a planned re-baseline of all 11 seeded nodes: 'Phase A ships `ctx.seed = (paramSeed) => paramSeed` — an identity derivation, declared as a deliberate Phase-C deferral with a named re-baseline.' Design 1 argues correctly that seeds are already params and no such migration is ever needed. Design 2 is paying for a migration it does not have to make.\n\nAlso says 'EXACT_TYPES (the hand-maintained 18-string Set at :4048)' — it is 19. A8's gate asserts equality with the 18-member literal and fails on arrival.\n\nStrong points beyond A1: it is the only design that spells out the oracle's hard failure modes as ASSERTIONS it read in the gate logic ('`if (primary.length !== RES*RES) → skipped`... a skipped type becomes `missing`... `missing.length` FAILS'), and correctly derives from that why `eval` must stay a plain writable own data property (`_verify_build_progress.js:21` patches `TYPES.blur.eval`) — a getter or `Object.freeze` breaks a gate silently. Neither rival states that constraint on `definePlugin`'s output.\n  DESIGN 1: Phase A: the shim-preserving plugin contract — definePlugin() emits a legacy TYPES entry, so _verify_digest.js is never edited: 6/10 - BEST-ARGUED INVARIANT, MOST ACCURATE MEASUREMENTS, AND IT SPENDS PHASE B IN PHASE A.\n\nIt has the sharpest single idea in the set: 'THE ORACLE FILE IS NOT EDITED IN ANY PHASE A COMMIT. That is the design goal, not a nicety: the moment the oracle and the app move in the same commit, \"60/60 green\" stops being evidence.' Its supporting measurement is the strongest evidence anyone produced — zero occurrences of `Object.keys(def)`, `Object.entries(def)`, `for..in def` or `JSON.stringify(def)` over a TYPES entry, so manifest growth is unobservable by construction. And it is the only design that got `EXACT_TYPES` right: I counted the literal at index.html:4048-4049 and it is 19 members, exactly as Design 1 states in all three places it cites it. Both rivals say 18 and both propose gates that assert 18.\n\nBut under the cost lens it fares worst, and for a reason the lens names explicitly.\n\nDOUBLE-TOUCH BY CONSTRUCTION. Step 7 populates the manifest for all 60 nodes ('but wire NONE of them'). Steps 10 and 11 then rewrite 49 of those same 60 bodies. Every node is edited twice in Phase A, 49 of them substantively, and the second edit is done 'in batches of ~8 so a drift names at most 8 suspects' — i.e. serially, by one operator, gated by a full digest run per batch. That is not Phase B made parallel; that is Phase B relabelled as Phase A and executed serially. The lens asks whether the 60 units can be parallelised, and this plan answers by pre-paying ~49 of them in sequence.\n\nTWO STRUCTURAL HOLES IN THE CONTRACT ITSELF.\n\n(1) NO AUX CONSUMPTION PATH. `Port` appears only in `writes`. `inPorts[].accepts: MapName` is declared on `inPorts`, but the design also asserts 'inPorts[i].label === ins[i], same length, same order' — so inPorts are exactly the existing 1-3 height/mask slots. There is no mechanism anywhere in the contract by which a downstream erosion node reads the soilDepth an upstream one wrote. ch27's first-class-citizen rule is a two-sided property — persistent fields travelling the DAG on typed ports — and this contract implements only the write side. Design 3 supplies `ctx.inputAux(slot, port)`; Design 2 supplies aux-as-inPort. Design 1 supplies neither, and the omission is not flagged.\n\n(2) THE SHIM CANNOT BIND COLORMIXER. `assertPortsMatchIns(spec)` is a static equality check and `bindPorts(spec, ins)` keys by the spec's static labels, with a fallback of `ports[spec.ins[0]]`. I read `makeNode` at index.html:4722-4726: colormixer's ports are INSTANCE state — `nd._inputs=[\"Layer 1\",\"Layer 2\",\"Layer 3\"]`, growing to 15, returned by `nodeInputs` in preference to `TYPES[t].ins`. So both the assertion and the binding are wrong for that node the first time a user adds a layer. The design's own risk section names the problem and then declines to solve it: 'Either the contract gains a variadic-port declaration and a param type for layer stacks, or these two stay hand-written... Say which, explicitly, rather than discovering it in Phase B.' It does not say which. Both rivals do (`variadicPorts`/`instanceState`; `variadic`/`'layerStack'`/`'strokes'`).\n\nA BOOT-TIME LANDMINE THAT SERIALISES PHASE B. `assertWritesExact(spec, out)` throws on a returned map that is not declared as well as on a declared map that is not returned — 'Missing => throw. Extra => throw.' In a plugin-per-node world that means one agent's mis-declared `writes` in one file takes the whole app down for every other agent mid-extraction. Throw on missing, warn on extra.\n\nCORRECT WHERE OTHERS ARE NOT: its RNG position. 'Every seed in this app is already a param... ctx.hash is the only randomness a plugin may touch', with the supporting argument that a stateful stream would make output depend on `evalGraphProgressive`'s cancellable rAF scheduling. That avoids the 11-node rootSeed re-baseline Design 2 explicitly plans for Phase C, and it is the cheaper and the more correct answer.\n\nIts step-1 gate ('`node _verify_bridge.js --check` must report zero uncovered symbols') cannot run: acorn is absent from package.json and node_modules. It adds the missing `bridge:gen` script but not the missing dependency.\nGRAFT: D3/A2 — the component-aware digest comparator (parse `parts.join('|')` at _verify_digest.js:411 into components; primary-drift=FAIL, component-removed=FAIL, component-added=REVIEW+re-baseline; add `--component=primary`; land it with the mutation drill that proves exactly one component of one node moves). Without this every Phase B/C aux implementation is a blind `--write` reviewed serially by a human who cannot distinguish addition from drift. This is the single highest-value item across all three plans. | D3 — aux maps travel on a side channel (`ctx.inputAux(slot, name)`) with `ins` order frozen permanently, NOT as new `ins` slots. The oracle seeds each slot distinctly (_verify_digest.js:198-254), so D2's aux-as-inPort route costs a digest re-baseline plus a saved-graph `edge.slot` migration for every node that gains a map. | D2/A1 — install `acorn` and add the `bridge:gen` script BEFORE anything else. Verified: acorn is in neither package.json nor node_modules, and _verify_bridge.js:33-44 FATALs. D1's step 1 and D3's A1 both gate on a command that cannot execute. Also take D2's un-breaking of `src/testing/bridge-block.js:8` (the DEV/test guard that kills `node _verify_digest.js dist/index.html`). | D1 — the RNG position: seeds stay params, `ctx.hash(x,y,seed)` is the only randomness, no rootSeed derivation is ever introduced. This deletes D2's planned Phase-C re-baseline of all 11 seeded nodes, and D1's justification (a stateful stream would couple output to evalGraphProgressive's cancellable rAF scheduling) is the correct reason. | D1 — the measurement that makes manifest growth provably free (zero `Object.keys(def)`/`Object.entries(def)`/`for..in def`/`JSON.stringify(def)` over a TYPES entry; complete read set is 11 properties), plus its recommendation that `_verify_manifest.js` assert no new enumeration of a TYPES entry has appeared. That assertion is what keeps the freedom. | D2 — `variadicPorts {min,max,template,layerParam}` and `instanceState` as DECLARED escape hatches for colormixer and drawmask, combined with D3's `'layerStack'`/`'strokes'` ParamDesc types. D1 names this gap and leaves it open; its `assertPortsMatchIns`/`bindPorts` are provably wrong against index.html:4722-4726. | D2 — the constraint that `definePlugin` must emit `eval` as a plain WRITABLE OWN DATA property, never a getter/Proxy/frozen object, because _verify_build_progress.js:21 monkey-patches `TYPES.blur.eval` and bridge-surface.json records TYPES as `patched`. Neither rival states this constraint on the generated entry. | D1's `assertWritesExact`, but softened: throw on a declared-and-not-returned map, WARN on a returned-and-not-declared one. As written ('Extra => throw') one agent's mis-declaration takes the app down for every other agent mid-extraction. | D3's optional ParamDesc fields (`unit?`, `invalidates?`, `quantise?`) in place of D2's mandatory `unit` + `invalidates`, which forces ~470 judgement calls across 235 declarations in one commit with nothing reading them. | MERGE THE PASSES: fold D3's A6 (wrap 60 in definePlugin) into A7 (manifest 60), and fold D1's step-7 manifest into each step-10/11 body batch, so no node is edited twice for reasons of commit hygiene alone. | D1's identity rules for the shim, verbatim, in whichever contract ships: inputs bound BY REFERENCE, `out.height` returned as the same instance, aux scattered as the same object at the same `_name`, fallback reproducing `ins[0]||newField()`. These protect FIELD_META (index.html:3202) and the four reference-identity caches, and the second of those four is invisible to the digest.\nFATAL: `node tests/legacy/_verify_bridge.js --check` CANNOT RUN. Verified: `acorn` is absent from apps/terrain-studio/package.json devDependencies and from node_modules; _verify_bridge.js:33-44 refuses a regex fallback and exits FATAL. Design 1 makes this the gate of its step 1 and Design 3 of its A1 — both plans' prerequisite commit is gated on a command that fails today. Only Design 2 caught it. Install acorn and add `bridge:gen` (generate-bridge.mjs:56 instructs you to run it; package.json has dev/preview/build/verify/test:e2e/test:e2e:ui and nothing else) before any other step in any of the three plans. | EXACT_TYPES HAS 19 MEMBERS, NOT 18. Read from index.html:4048-4049: perlin, simplex, ridged, worley, gradient, shape, drawmask, constant, levels, curve, clampn, invert, terrace, blend, add, maxmin, smin, smax, stampn. Design 2's A8 and Design 3's A7 both propose a gate asserting the manifest-derived set equals 'the 18-member literal' / 'the 18 members of EXACT_TYPES'. Both gates fail on arrival. Design 1 is correct at 19. (The miscount is itself the argument for moving the hand-maintained Set into the manifest.) | DESIGN 3'S `evaluate(ctx: EvalContext): Outputs` GIVES A PLUGIN NO WAY TO READ ITS OWN PARAMETERS. The declared EvalContext members are nodeId, typeId, res, rows, len, lattice, cellSizeM, resScale, quality, xf, world, L, newField, withResolution, withTransform, input, inputAux, inputMeta, canEvaluateExact, evaluateUpstream, rootSeed, rng, device, gpu, ledger, progress, cancelled — no `params`. The design even types `scalingPolicy?: (p: Params, ctx: EvalContext) => Params`, referencing a `Params` the eval entry point never receives. This is the signature all 60 plugins are written against. | DESIGN 1 HAS NO PATH FOR A NODE TO READ AN UPSTREAM AUXILIARY MAP. `Port` occurs only under `writes`; `inPorts` is asserted to be label-for-label identical to today's `ins`, i.e. the same 1-3 height/mask slots. ch27's first-class-citizen rule requires persistent typed fields travelling THROUGH the DAG; Design 1 implements the producer half only. A downstream erosion node can never consume the soilDepth an upstream node was forced to declare and return. The design does not flag the omission. | DESIGN 1'S SHIM BREAKS ON COLORMIXER. `assertPortsMatchIns(spec)` asserts static `inPorts[i].label === ins[i]` with equal length, and `bindPorts(spec, ins)` keys by the spec's static labels with a `ports[spec.ins[0]]` fallback. Verified at index.html:4722-4726: makeNode assigns `nd._inputs=[\"Layer 1\",\"Layer 2\",\"Layer 3\"]`, growing to 15, and nodeInputs (:4050) returns `nd._inputs` in preference to `TYPES[t].ins`. Ports are instance state for that node. Design 1's own risk text says 'Say which, explicitly, rather than discovering it in Phase B' and then does not say which — this must be resolved with a variadic declaration before the shim ships. | DESIGN 1'S `assertWritesExact` THROWS ON EXTRA KEYS ('Missing => throw. Extra => throw.'), AT REGISTRATION/EVAL TIME. In a plugin-per-node repo one agent's over-declared or under-declared `writes` in one file takes the whole app down for every other agent mid-extraction — a boot-time landmine that serialises the exact phase the design is trying to parallelise. Throw on missing; warn on extra."
